<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:BGK_sol_space}
For $\gamma>0$, $t_0>0$, and given inflow boundary data $g(t,x_h,v)$, $\Omega_{\gamma, t_0}$ is the collection of functions $f(t,x,v)\geq 0$ for $0\leq t\leq t_0$ such that
\begin{enumerate}
    \item it satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0< t\leq t_0}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+}\int_{v\in \mathbb{R}^n}\left(1+\left(|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{2\gamma} + \left(v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)^2\right)f(t,x,v)\,dv<\infty
    \end{align*}
    and $f(0,x,v) = 0$.
    \item We define the macroscopic fields by
    \begin{align*}
        \rho(t,x)(1, u(t,x), nT(t,x)) &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (1, v, |v-u(t,x)|^2) f(t,x,v)\,dv,\\
        \rho_g(t,x)(1, u_g(t,x), nT_g(t,x)) &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (1, v, |v-u_g(t,x)|^2) e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b, x - vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\,dv,
    \end{align*}
    where $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$ are the macroscopic fields generated by the damped free transport of the inflow boundary data $e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b, x - vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}$. Then, there exists a constant $C_u>0$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:X_T_A3}
        \begin{split}
            &|\rho-\rho_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}\rho_g(t,x),\\
            &\left|u-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|(t,x)\leq C_u\sqrt{T_g(t,x)},\\
            &|T-T_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}T_g(t,x)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for almost every $0<t\leq t_0$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main_BGK}
    Let $n\geq 2$, $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$, $\alpha>0$, $1\leq \beta\leq 2$, $\delta\geq 0$, and assume vacuum initial data $f_0(x,v) = 0$. For any $\epsilon>0$, there exists inflow boundary data $g(t,x_h,v)$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{3_thm:main_BGK_1_cond}
        \begin{split}
            &\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left\|w^h_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\right\|_{L_v^p(\mathbb{R}^n, L_x^q(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}<\epsilon,\\
            &\left(\text{resp. }\sup_{0\leq t<\infty} \left\|w^h_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\right\|_{L_x^q(\mathbb{R}^n_+, L_v^p(\mathbb{R}^n))}<\epsilon,\right)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    but there exists a unique solution of the BGK model $f(t,x,v)$ in $\Omega_{\frac{1}{1+\beta}, t_0}$ for some $t_0$ under the boundary data $g$ satisfying
    \begin{align}\label{3_thm:main_BGK_1}
        \begin{split}
            &\|w^h_{\alpha',\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\|_{L_v^p(\mathbb{R}^n, L_x^q(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}=\infty\\
            &\left(\text{resp. }\|w^h_{\alpha',\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\|_{L_x^q(\mathbb{R}^n_+, L_v^p(\mathbb{R}^n))}=\infty\right)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for any $0<\alpha'\leq \alpha$ and $0<t\leq t_0$. Furthermore, for any solution $\tilde{f}(t,x,v)$ in $t\in [0, t^*_0]$ with $t^*_0>t_0$, if $\tilde{f}(t,x,v) = f(t,x,v)$ for $0\leq t\leq t_0$, it still satisfies \eqref{3_thm:main_BGK_1} for $0<t\leq t^*_0$. Here, $\tilde{f}(t,x,v)$ does not need to be in $\Omega_{\frac{1}{1+\beta}, t_0^*}$.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:main_Boltzmann}
    For the collision kernel given by \eqref{eq:B_condition}, let $n\geq 2$, $\alpha>0$, $\max\left\{0, \frac{2}{n-1}\kappa, \frac{2}{3}(1+\kappa)\right\}<\beta\leq 2$, and $\delta>n+1$. If $n=2$, we additionally assume that $1+\kappa\leq \beta$. Given any $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$, the following holds. For the vacuum initial data $f_0(x,v) = 0$ and an inflow boundary datum $g(t,x_h,v)$ for $v_z>0$ satisfying
    \begin{align}\label{3_thm:main_Boltzmann_1_cond}
        \begin{split}
            w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}g(t, x_h, v)&\in L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}\left([0, t^*_0]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n\right),\\
            v_z^{1/q}w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}g(t, x_h, v)&\in L^p_v\left(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t, x_h}([0, t^*_0]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+)\right)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for some $t_0^*>0$, there exists a unique solution of the Boltzmann equation satisfying
    \begin{align*}
        w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\in L^\infty_{loc, t}\left([0, t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n)\cap L^p_v\left(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+)\right)\right)
    \end{align*}
    for some $0<t_0\leq t^*_0$. The $t_0$ depends on $\alpha$, $\beta$, $\delta$, $n$, $\kappa$, and $\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}g(t, x_h, v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}\left([0, t^*_0]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n\right)}$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
For a function $\phi(t,x_h,v)$ for $x_h\in\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$ and $\phi = 0$ for $v_z\leq 0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \|\phi(t-t_b, x_h-v_ht_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}=\|v_z^{1/q} \phi(\tau,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for any $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$. Therefore, the norm in \eqref{3_thm:main_Boltzmann_1_cond} corresponds to the $L_v^p(\mathbb{R}^n, L_x^q(\mathbb{R}^n_+))$ norm in \eqref{3_thm:main_BGK_1_cond} except the weight function $w^h_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}$ and $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}$.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
For technical reasons, we consider the $L^p_v\left(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+)\right)$ norm for the Boltzmann equation, which corresponds to the $L^p_vL^q_x$ case of Theorem \ref{thm:main_BGK}. If we use Minkowski's integral inequality for $q\geq p$, we can check $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\in L^\infty_{loc, t}\left([0, t_0), L^q_x\left(\mathbb{R}^n_+, L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n)\right)\right)$ under the assumption \eqref{3_thm:main_Boltzmann_1_cond}.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:mild_sol_equiv}
    If $A(t,x,v),B(t,x,v)\in L^1_{loc}([0,t_0]\times \overline{\mathbb{R}^n_+}\times \mathbb{R}_v^n)$, then \eqref{eq:mild_def} and \eqref{eq:mild_def_2} are equivalent, where $\overline{\mathbb{R}^n_+}$ is the closure of $\mathbb{R}^n_+$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We prove the direction $\eqref{eq:mild_def}\Rightarrow \eqref{eq:mild_def_2}$, and the opposite direction follows by reversing the proof. We first extend the $f(t,x,v)$ on $\mathbb{R}\times \mathbb{R}^n\times \mathbb{R}^n$ by choosing $f(t,x,v) = 0$ if $(t,x,v)\not\in [0,t_0]\times\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n$. We introduce an auxiliary variable $w\in\mathbb{R}$ and consider $f(t+w, x+vw, v)$. From \eqref{eq:mild_def}, we obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:mild_def_1_1}
        \begin{split}
            &f(t+w, x+vw, v) \\
            &= e^{-\int_0^{t_b(x+vw,v)} A(t-(\tau-w), x-v(\tau-w), v)\,d\tau}g(t+w-t_b(x+vw,v), x+vw-vt_b(x+vw,v), v)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v)\leq t+w}\\
            &\quad + e^{-\int_0^{t+w} A(t-(\tau-w), x-v(\tau-w), v)\,d\tau}f(0, x-vt, v)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v)>t+w}\\
            &\quad + \int_0^{t+w} e^{-\int_0^\tau A(t-(s-w), x-v(s-w), v)\,ds}B(t-(\tau-w), x-v(\tau-w), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(\tau-w)>0}\,d\tau
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    when $0\leq t+w\leq t_0$ and $z+v_zw>0$, while $f(t+w, x+vw, v) = 0$ otherwise. We note that \eqref{eq:mild_def} and \eqref{eq:mild_def_1_1} are equivalent in a.e. sense as
    \begin{align}\label{eq:a.e._equiv}
        \begin{split}
            &\iint_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times \mathbb{R}^n\times \mathbb{R}^n}\left|f(t+w, x+vw, v) - (\text{RHS of \eqref{eq:mild_def_1_1}})\right|\mathbf{1}_{0<t+w<t_0}\mathbf{1}_{z+v_zw>0}\,dtdwdxdv\\
            &=\iint_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times \mathbb{R}^n\times \mathbb{R}^n}\left|f(t, x, v) - (\text{RHS of \eqref{eq:mild_def}})\right|\mathbf{1}_{0<t<t_0}\mathbf{1}_{z>0}\,dtdwdxdv
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    by the change of variables.

    For technical use, we claim that
    \begin{align*}
        \dv{w} \left(t_b(x+vw, v) - w\right) = 0
    \end{align*}
    for $v$ and $w$ satisfying $z+v_zw>0$ and $v_z>0$, so $t_b(x+vw, v)\neq \infty$. Indeed, for any small $h$ making $z+v_z(w+h)>0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        t_b(x+v(w+h), v) - (w + h) = t_b(x+vw, v) + h - (w + h) = t_b(x+vw, v) - w,
    \end{align*}
    so it is a constant function.

    Using this identity, we rewrite \eqref{eq:mild_def_1_1} as
    \begin{align}\label{eq:+w}
        \begin{split}
            &f(t+w, x+vw, v) \\
            &= e^{-\int_{-w}^{t_b(x+vw,v) - w} A(t-\tau, x-v\tau, v)\,d\tau}g\left(t-(t_b(x+vw,v)-w), x-v(t_b(x+vw,v)-w), v\right)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v) - w\leq t}\\
            &\quad + e^{-\int_{-w}^t A(t-\tau, x-v\tau, v)\,d\tau}f(0, x-vt, v)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v) - w>t}\\
            &\quad + \int_{-w}^t e^{-\int_{-w}^\tau A(t-s, x-vs, v)\,ds}B(t-\tau, x-v\tau, v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z\tau>0}\,d\tau.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Since $A(t,x,v)$ and $B(t,x,v)$ are locally integrable, by Fubini's theorem, the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:+w} is differentiable for a.e. $w$ satisfying $0<t+w<t_0$ and $z+v_zw>0$, and
    \begin{align*}
        \dv{w}\left(\text{R.H.S}\right) = -A(t+w, x+vw, v) f(t+w, x+vw, v) + B(t+w, x+vw, v)\mathbf{1}_{z+v_zw>0}.
    \end{align*}
    Integrating both sides by $w$, we get
    \begin{align*}
        f(t+w, x+vw, v)&=g(t+w-t_b(x+vw,v), x+vw-vt_b(x+vw,v), v)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v)\leq t+w}\\
        &\quad + f(0, x-vt, v)\mathbf{1}_{t_b(x+vw,v)>t+w}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^{t+w} B(t+w-\tau, x-v(\tau-w), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(\tau-w)>0} - A f(t+w-\tau, x-v(\tau-w), v)\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    As $f(t+w,x+vw,v) = 0$ if $x+vw\not\in \mathbb{R}^n_+$, the final term is same as
    \begin{align*}
        \int_0^{t+w} \left(B(t+w-\tau, x-v(\tau-w), v)- Af(t+w-\tau, x-v(\tau-w), v)\right)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(\tau-w)>0}\,d\tau
    \end{align*}
    Since the equality holds for a.e. $(t,x,v)$ and $w$ for $0<t+w<t_0$ and $z+v_zw>0$, the same argument as in \eqref{eq:a.e._equiv} yields \eqref{eq:mild_def_2} for a.e. $(t,x,v)$. Conversely, \eqref{eq:mild_def} follows from \eqref{eq:mild_def_2} by reversing the preceding argument.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:trace}
    (Well-definedness of the trace) Let $g_1(t,x_h,v)$ and $g_2(t,x_h,v)$ be measurable functions on $[0, \infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n_+$. Then, $g_1(t,x_h,v) = g_2(t,x_h,v)$ for a.e. $(t,x,v)\in [0,\infty)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n_+$ if and only if for any $t> 0$,
    \begin{align*}
        g_1(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b} = g_2(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}
    \end{align*}
    for a.e. $(x,v)$.
    Furthermore, if $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$ with notation $1/\infty = 0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \|g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}=\|v_z^{1/q} g(\tau,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for any $t> 0$.
    Equivalently,
    \begin{align*}
        \|e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}=e^{-t}\|v_z^{1/q} e^\tau g(\tau,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for any $t>0$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
The first statement is easily checked by taking the integral of $|g_1-g_2|$. For fixed $t$, writing $t_b = \frac{z}{v_z}\geq 0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n_+} |g_1(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)-g_2(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)|\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\,dxdv \\
        &=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}_+\times\mathbb{R}^n_+} |g_1(t-\frac{z}{v_z}, x-v\frac{z}{v_z}, v)-g_2(t-\frac{z}{v_z}, x-v\frac{z}{v_z}, v)|\mathbf{1}_{t\geq \frac{z}{v_z}}\,dx_h dz dv \\
        &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}_+\times\mathbb{R}^n_+} v_z|g_1(t-t_b, x_h, v)-g_2(t-t_b, x_h, v)|\mathbf{1}_{0\leq t_b\leq t}\,dx_hdt_b dv\\
        &= \int_0^t \left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}^n_+} v_z|g_1(t_b, x_h, v)-g_2(t_b, x_h, v)|\,dx_h dv\right)\,dt_b.
    \end{align*}
    Since the integrands are nonnegative and $v_z>0$ almost everywhere, the identity above shows that, for each $t>0$, the transported functions agree almost everywhere on $\mathbb{R}_+^n\times\mathbb{R}_+^n$ if and only if $g_1=g_2$ almost everywhere on $[0,t]\times\partial\mathbb{R}_+^n\times\mathbb{R}_+^n$. Since $t>0$ is arbitrary, the first assertion follows.

    For the second statement, we first suppose $1\leq q<\infty$. Then,
    \begin{align*}
        \int \left|g\left(t-\frac{z}{v_z}, x-v\frac{z}{v_z}, v\right)\right|^q \mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\,dx &= v_z\int |g(t-t_b, x_h, v)|^q \mathbf{1}_{0\leq t_b\leq t}\,dx_h dt_b \\
        &= v_z \|g(t_b,x_h,v)\|^q_{L^q_{t_b, x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+)}
    \end{align*}
    for any $t>0$. Therefore, we have the lemma for $1\leq q<\infty$. The $q=\infty$ case follows from the pointwise estimate. For the final one, we choose $g(\tau, x, v)\mapsto e^\tau g(\tau, x, v)$ in the second result.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:exp_integral}
Let $n\geq 1$, $\alpha>0$, $0< \beta\leq 2$, $\delta_1\geq 0$, $\delta_2\in\mathbb{R}$, and $M\geq 0$. Then, there exist constants $C_1$ and $C_2$ depending on $n$, $\alpha$, $\beta$, $\delta_1$, and $\delta_2$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        C_1\leq \frac{1}{\langle M\rangle^{(n-\beta) + (1-\beta)\delta_1 + \delta_2} e^{-\alpha M^\beta}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v|-M\right)^{\delta_1}\langle v\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha|v|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v|\geq M}\,dv\leq C_2.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Using the spherical coordinates, we write
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (|v|-M)^{\delta_1}\langle v\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha|v|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v|\geq M}\,dv = |\mathbb{S}^{n-1}|\int_M^\infty (r-M)^{\delta_1}\langle r\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha r^\beta}r^{n-1}\,dr.
    \end{align*}
    We further use the change of variable $r^\beta\mapsto r$. Then, we get
    \begin{align}\label{eq:ul_rm}
        \int_M^\infty (r-M)^{\delta_1}\langle r\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha r^\beta}r^{n-1}\,dr = \frac{1}{\beta}\int_{M^\beta}^\infty \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1}\big\langle r^{1/\beta} \big\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha r} r^{\frac{n}{\beta}- 1}\,dr.
    \end{align}
    If $M^\beta\leq 10$, the integral is bounded above and below by positive constants. Thus, we safely assume $M^\beta\geq 10$. For every $x\geq 10$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        x\leq \langle x\rangle \leq 2x,
    \end{align*}
    so we can replace the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:ul_rm} by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{M^\beta}^\infty \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr.
    \end{align*}
    
    To estimate this integral, we partition the interval $\left[M^\beta, \infty\right)$ by setting
    $r_i := M^\beta + i$ for $i \in \mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}$, and estimate the integral on each sub-interval $[r_i, r_{i+1})$. To deal with the term $\left(r^{1/\beta} -  M\right)^{\delta_1}$, we put $r=r_i$ and write
    \begin{align*}
        r_i^{1/\beta} -  M &=\left(M^\beta + i\right)^{1/\beta} -  M = M\left((1+iM^{-\beta})^{1/\beta}-1\right).
    \end{align*}
    For $0\leq x\leq 1$ and $0< \beta\leq 2$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        1+\frac{x}{2\beta}\leq (1+x)^{1/\beta}\leq 1+\frac{\max\{2^{1/\beta - 1}, 1\}}{\beta}x
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, if $0\leq i\leq M^\beta$, we obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:M_bounds}
        \begin{split}
            M\left((1+iM^{-\beta})^{1/\beta}-1\right)&\geq i\frac{M^{1-\beta}}{2\beta}\\
            M\left((1+iM^{-\beta})^{1/\beta}-1\right)&\leq i\frac{\max\{2^{1/\beta - 1}, 1\}}{\beta}M^{1-\beta}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    We first derive the lower bound of the integral by
    \begin{align}
        &\int_{M^\beta}^\infty \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\geq \int_{M^\beta}^{2M^\beta + 1} \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\notag\\
        &\geq \sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor M^\beta\rfloor} \left(i\frac{M^{1-\beta}}{2\beta}\right)^{\delta_1}\min\left\{(M^\beta+i)^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (M^\beta+(i+1))^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\} e^{-\alpha(M^\beta + (i+1))}\notag\\
        &=(2\beta)^{-\delta_1}M^{(1-\beta)\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n - \beta}e^{-\alpha M^\beta}\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor M^\beta\rfloor} i^{\delta_1}\min\left\{(1+iM^{-\beta})^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (1+(i+1)M^{-\beta})^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\} e^{-\alpha(i+1)}.\label{eq:lower}
    \end{align}
    We need to show that the summation in \eqref{eq:lower} is bounded below by a positive constant independent of $M$. If $\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1\geq 0$, then the minimum term is not smaller than $1$. Hence, it is lower bounded by $\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor M^\beta\rfloor} i^{\delta_1}e^{-\alpha(i+1)}$. On the other hand, if $\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1<0$, then, since $M^\beta\geq 10$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        i^{\delta_1}\min\left\{(1+iM^{-\beta})^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (1+(i+1)M^{-\beta})^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\}\geq i^{\delta_1}(1+(i+1))^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1} e^{-\alpha(i+1)}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, the summation is lower bounded by a positive constant independent of $M$. Combining the two cases yields
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v|-M\right)^{\delta_1}\langle v\rangle^{\delta_2} e^{-\alpha|v|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v|\geq M}\,dv\geq C_{\alpha,\beta,\delta,n}\langle M\rangle ^{(1-\beta)\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n - \beta}e^{-\alpha M^\beta}.
    \end{align*}
    
    For the upper bound, we split the integral region into $[M^\beta, 2M^\beta - 1)$ and $[2M^\beta - 1, \infty)$. For the integral on $[M^\beta, 2M^\beta - 1)$, we take the same partitioning by $[r_i, r_{i+1})$ as above. Then, using \eqref{eq:M_bounds}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{M^\beta}^{2M^\beta - 1} \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\\
        &\leq \sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor M^\beta\rfloor} \left((i+1)\frac{\max\{2^{1/\beta - 1}, 1\}}{\beta}M^{1-\beta}\right)^{\delta_1}\max\left\{(M^\beta+i)^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (M^\beta+(i+1))^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\} e^{-\alpha(M^\beta + i)}.
    \end{align*}
    For the summation, we apply the same argument as in the lower bound estimate and get
    \begin{align}\label{eq:2_3:upper_1}
        \begin{split}
            &\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor M^\beta\rfloor} \left((i+1)\frac{\max\{2^{1/\beta - 1}, 1\}}{\beta}M^{1-\beta}\right)^{\delta_1}\max\left\{(M^\beta+i)^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (M^\beta+(i+1))^{\frac{\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\} e^{-\alpha(M^\beta + i)}\\
            &\leq C_{\alpha,\beta,\delta,n}\langle M\rangle ^{(1-\beta)\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n - \beta}e^{-\alpha M^\beta}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    For the integral on $[2M^\beta - 1, \infty)$, we crudely bound the polynomial part by $(r^{1/\beta}-M)^{\delta_1}\leq r^{\delta_1/\beta}$. Partitioning this region by $r_i = 2M^\beta + i$ for $i\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{-1, 0\}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{2M^\beta - 1}^\infty \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\leq \int_{2M^\beta - 1}^\infty r^{\frac{\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\\
        &\leq \sum_{i=-1}^\infty \max\left\{(2M^\beta+i)^{\frac{\delta_1+\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}, (2M^\beta+(i+1))^{\frac{\delta_1+\delta_2+n}{\beta}-1}\right\} e^{-\alpha(2M^\beta + i)}\\
        &\leq C_{\alpha,\beta,\delta,n}\langle M\rangle^{\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n - \beta}e^{-2\alpha M^\beta}.
    \end{align*}
    Since the exponential decay is much stronger than \eqref{eq:2_3:upper_1}, we get
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{M^\beta}^\infty \left(r^{1/\beta}-M\right)^{\delta_1} r^{\frac{\delta_2 + n}{\beta}- 1} e^{-\alpha r} \,dr\leq C_{\alpha,\beta,\delta,n}\langle M\rangle ^{(1-\beta)\delta_1 + \delta_2 + n - \beta}e^{-\alpha M^\beta}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:beta_pert}
    For $x\geq 1$, the following inequalities hold.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item If $1\leq \beta\leq 3$ and $0\leq c\leq x^\beta$, then
        \begin{align*}
            (x+c x^{1-\beta})^\beta\leq x^\beta +4\beta c.
        \end{align*}
        \item If $1\leq \beta\leq 3$ and $-\frac{x^\beta}{2}\leq c\leq 0$, then
        \begin{align*}
            (x+c x^{1-\beta})^\beta\leq x^\beta +\frac{\beta}{4}c.
        \end{align*}
        \item For $1\leq \beta\leq 3$ and $-x^{\beta}\leq c$,
        \begin{align*}
            x^\beta +\beta c\leq (x+c x^{1-\beta})^\beta.
        \end{align*}
        \item If $0\leq \beta\leq 1$ and $-x^\beta\leq c\leq 0$, then
        \begin{align*}
            x^\beta + c\leq (x + c x^{1-\beta})^\beta.
        \end{align*}
    \end{enumerate}
    If $0\leq \beta \leq 1$ and $x,y> 0$, then
    \begin{align}\label{eq:beta_pert_5}
        (x+y)^\beta\leq \min\left\{x^\beta + \beta y x^{\beta-1}, y^\beta + \beta x y^{\beta-1}\right\}.
    \end{align}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
For the first to fourth inequalities, we have
    \begin{align*}
        (x+cx^{1-\beta})^\beta = x^\beta(1+cx^{-\beta})^\beta.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, we can rewrite the inequalities as
    \begin{align}
        (1+x)^\beta&\leq 1 + 4\beta x\quad \text{for}\quad 1\leq \beta\leq 3,\quad 0\leq x\leq 1,\label{eq:beta_pert_1}\\
        (1+x)^\beta&\leq 1 + \frac{\beta}{4} x\quad \text{for}\quad 1\leq \beta\leq 3,\quad -\frac{1}{2}\leq x\leq 0,\label{eq:beta_pert_2}\\
        (1+x)^\beta&\geq 1 + \beta x\quad \text{for}\quad 1\leq \beta\leq 3,\quad -1\leq x,\label{eq:beta_pert_3}\\
        (1+x)^\beta&\geq 1 + x\quad \text{for}\quad 0\leq \beta\leq 1,\quad -1\leq x\leq 0\label{eq:beta_pert_4}.
    \end{align}
    
    The inequality \eqref{eq:beta_pert_4} is trivial inequality when $0\leq x+1\leq 1$ and $0\leq \beta\leq 1$. So, we prove the first to third inequalities in steps. For the first inequality, we first check that
    \begin{align*}
        \beta (1+x)^{\beta-1}-4\beta\leq 0
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq \beta\leq 3$ and $0\leq x\leq 1$. Integrating the inequality for $0\leq x\leq 1$, we have \eqref{eq:beta_pert_1}. For the second and third inequalities, we similarly observe that
    \begin{align*}
        \beta(1+x)^{\beta-1} - \frac{\beta}{4}\geq 0,\quad -\frac{1}{2}\leq x\leq 0,
    \end{align*}
    and
    \begin{align*}
        \beta(1+x)^{\beta-1} - \beta\begin{cases}
            \geq 0 & x\geq 0,\\
            \leq 0 & -1< x\leq 0
        \end{cases}
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq \beta\leq 3$. Therefore, applying the same argument, we obtain \eqref{eq:beta_pert_2} and \eqref{eq:beta_pert_3}.

    For the final one \eqref{eq:beta_pert_5}, it is sufficient to show that
    \begin{align*}
        (x+y)^\beta\leq x^\beta + \beta y x^{\beta-1}
    \end{align*}
    by the exchange symmetry $x\leftrightarrow y$. We first homogenize the inequality by $t =\frac{y}{x}$. Then, it is equivalent to show
    \begin{align*}
        (1+t)^\beta\leq \beta t + 1.
    \end{align*}
    Differentiating with respect to $t$, we get
    \begin{align*}
        \beta (1+t)^{\beta-1}-\beta \leq 0
    \end{align*}
    for $t>0$, and the inequality is true when $t=0$. Therefore, it holds for $t\geq 0$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:gamma_pert}
    Assume that $2\leq \gamma\leq 3$, $x,C\geq 0$, $0\leq t\leq \frac{1}{4}$, and $x+C\geq 1$. If $r\geq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$, then
    \begin{align*}
        r\geq ((x+rt)+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}.
    \end{align*}
    If $r\leq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$, then
    \begin{align*}
        r\leq (|x-rt|+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Without loss of generality, we assume $t>0$. We first apply Lemma \ref{lem:beta_pert} to obtain
    \begin{align}
        \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma&\geq (x+C) + 2t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}},\label{eq:gamma_pert_1}\\
        \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma&\leq (x+C) - t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}.\label{eq:gamma_pert_2}
    \end{align}
    Starting from these facts, we will show that $r = (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$ (resp. $r = (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$) fulfills the first (resp. second) inequality. After that, we enlarge the region such that each inequality holds.
    
    For the first inequality in the lemma, we need to verify
    \begin{align*}
        r^\gamma - (x+r t) - C\geq 0.
    \end{align*}
    The left-hand side is an increasing function when $r\geq (t/\gamma)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}$, so when $r\geq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$ as $x+C\geq 1$. Using \eqref{eq:gamma_pert_1}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma - \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right) t - (x + C)\\
        &\geq t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - t^2(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}} = t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\left(1 - t(x+C)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)\\
        &\geq 0.
    \end{align*}
    It means that $r\geq ((x+rt)+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}$ when $r\geq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + 2\frac{t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$.

    For the second inequality, we need to check
    \begin{align*}
        r^\gamma - |x - r t| - C\leq 0.
    \end{align*}
    We divide into two cases: $(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\leq \frac{x}{t}$ and the other case. For the former case, when $r\leq \frac{x}{t}$, then the inequality is same as
    \begin{align}\label{eq:gamma_pert_2_1}
        r^\gamma - x + r t - C\leq 0.
    \end{align}
    If $r= (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$, we obtain from \eqref{eq:gamma_pert_2} that
    \begin{align*}
        &\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma + \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)t -x - C\\
        &\leq (x+C) - t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} + \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)t -x - C\\
        &\leq 0.
    \end{align*}
    Since left-hand side of \eqref{eq:gamma_pert_2_1} is an increasing function of $r$, the inequality holds whenever $r\leq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$.

    Next, we assume $(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}> \frac{x}{t}$. Then, we need to check the inequality
    \begin{align*}
        r^\gamma + x - r t - C\leq 0
    \end{align*}
    for $\frac{x}{t}\leq r<(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$; since the original inequality is an increasing function for $r\leq \frac{x}{t}$, if it is negative at $r = \frac{x}{t}$, it still holds for $r\leq \frac{x}{t}$. First, when $r = (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma - \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)t + x - C\\
        &\leq (x+C) - t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)t + x - C\\
        &=2x - 2t\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{2t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)\\
        &\leq 2t\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right) - 2t\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{2t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)\leq 0
    \end{align*}
    as $x<t\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)$.

    Next, we put $r = \frac{x}{t}$ and verify
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^\gamma - C\leq 0.
    \end{align*}
    We claim that $\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^\gamma>C$ can not happen. If this were the case, then
    \begin{align*}
        C< \left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^\gamma &<\left((x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}\right)^\gamma\\
        &\leq (x+C) - t(x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}},
    \end{align*}
    so
    \begin{align*}
        (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}<\frac{x}{t}.
    \end{align*}
    It contradicts the assumption. Therefore, we get $\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^\gamma - C\leq 0$.

    Finally, as $r^\gamma + x - r t - C$ is a convex function in $r$, we get $r^\gamma + x - r t - C\leq 0$ for $\frac{x}{t}\leq r\leq (x+C)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}} - \frac{4t}{\gamma}(x+C)^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma}}$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:concaten}
    Let $\beta>0$ and $C>0$ be constants. For $x,y>0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        x^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} + \frac{y^2}{Cx^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\geq (x+y)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} - \left((1+C)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} + \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C\right).
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
If $y\leq Cx^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}$, then by Lemma~\ref{lem:beta_pert} (for $x\geq 1$), we have
    \begin{align*}
        (x+y)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} - (1+C)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} - \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C&\leq (x+Cx^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}})^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} - (1+C)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}- \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C\\
        &\leq \begin{cases}
            (1+C)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}- (1+C)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}& x\leq 1\\
            x^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}+\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C  - \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C& x\geq 1
        \end{cases}\\
        &\leq x^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}.
    \end{align*}
    If $y\geq Cx^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}$, then applying Lemma~\ref{lem:beta_pert} again, we get
    \begin{align*}
        (x+y)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} - x^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}\leq \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}yx^{-\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\leq \frac{y^2}{Cx^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}
    \end{align*}
    as
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}C x^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\leq y.
    \end{align*}
    This proves the inequality.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{Def:macro_0}
    For $t> 0$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$, we define $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$ by
    \begin{align*}
        \rho_g(t,x)\left(1, u_g(t,x), nT_g(t,x) + u_g^2(t,x)\right) &\coloneqq \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (1,v,|v|^2) e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\,dv\\
        &= e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (1,v,|v|^2) g_0(x_h-v_ht, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:boundary_int}
    For $\alpha>0$, $1\leq\beta\leq 2$, $m\geq 0$, we set the inflow boundary data $g$ by \eqref{eq:boundary_data}. Then,
    \begin{align}\label{eq:macro_0_bound}
        \begin{split}
            &C_1\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\leq \rho_g(t,x)\leq C_2\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}},\\
            &\left|u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq C_3\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\\
            &C_4\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}} \leq T_g(t,x)\leq C_5\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $0< t\leq \frac{1}{4}$, where $C_i>0$, $1\leq i\leq 5$, are constants depending only on $\alpha$, $\beta$, $m$, and dimension $n$. Moreover, for given $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$ and $\delta\geq 0$, suppose $m$ satisfies
    \begin{align}\label{eq:m_cond1}
        \frac{n+\beta}{p}+(1+\beta)\left(\delta + \frac{n}{q}\right)<m
    \end{align}
    with the convention $1/\infty =0$. Then, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{-t_b}e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right\|_{L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+, L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n))}\leq C t^{1/q}(1+t^2)^{n/q}e^{-t},
    \end{align*}
    and
    \begin{align*}
        &\left\|\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{-t_b}e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\\
        &=e^{-t}\left\|v_z^{1/q}e^\tau\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(\tau,x_h,v)\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^{n}_+))}\leq Ct^{1/q}e^{-t}
    \end{align*}
    for all $t\geq 0$, where the constant $C$ depends on $\alpha$, $\beta$, $m$, $n$, $p$, $q$, and $\delta$.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We first compute the macroscopic fields $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$ step by step and then prove finiteness in the exponentially weighted norm. The main difficulty in estimating the macroscopic fields lies in handling the cutoff functions
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left||x_h-v_ht| + v_z\right|^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\qquad \text{and}\qquad \mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b} = \mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}.
    \end{align*}\\
    
    \noindent \textbullet{} \textit{$\rho_g$ computation.} Throughout the computation, it is convenient to employ the change of variable $v_z\mapsto v_z + \frac{z}{t}$, so we write
    \begin{align*}
        \rho_g(t,x) = e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} g_0(x_h-v_ht, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv = e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha |v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    Using the elementary bound
    \begin{align*}
        \left||x_h| - |v_h|t\right|\leq |x_h-v_ht|\leq |x_h|+|v_h|t,
    \end{align*}
    we bound $\rho_g(t,x)$ as follows:
    \begin{align*}
        \rho_g(t,x) &\leq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(\left||x_h| - |v_h| t\right|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z,
        \intertext{and}
        \rho_g(t,x)&\geq e^{-\frac{1}{4}}\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left((|x_h| + |v_h| t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z
    \end{align*}
    for $0< t\leq \frac{1}{4}$. To employ Lemma~\ref{lem:gamma_pert}, we divide cases depending on the size of $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}$.\\
    
    \noindent \textbf{(a)} If $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}<1$, then we further divide $v_z$ into $0\leq v_z\leq 1$ and $v_z\geq 1$. We first assume $0\leq v_z\leq 1$. If $|v_h|= 2$ and $t\leq \frac{1}{4}$, then
    \begin{align*}
        ((|x_h| + |v_h| t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\leq \left(\frac{1}{2}+2\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq \beta\leq 2$. It implies that $|v_h|\geq ((|x_h| + |v_h| t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}$ for $|v_h|\geq 2$. Therefore, we get
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(\left||x_h| - |v_h| t\right|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_hdv_z\leq \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\,dv_hdv_z\leq C'_1,
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left((|x_h|+|v_h|t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_hdv_z\geq \int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq 2}\,dv_hdv_z\geq C'_2
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C'_1$ and $C'_2$.
    
    If $v_z\geq 1$, using Lemma~\ref{lem:gamma_pert} for $C = \frac{z}{t} + v_z$, we can take the upper and lower bounds of the step function by
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(\left||x_h|-|v_h|t\right|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z \\
        &\leq \int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z,
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left((|x_h|+|v_h|t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z \\
        &\geq \int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z
    \end{align*}
    since $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+v_z\geq 1$ and $0<t\leq \frac{1}{4}$. As $|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1$, each integral is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq v_z^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}(1 + v_z)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z\leq C''_2,\\
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_1^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (1 + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}(1+v_z)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z\geq C''_1
    \end{align*}
    for some uniform constants $C_1''$ and $C_2''$. Combining the two cases $0\leq v_z\leq 1$ and $v_z\geq 1$, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:rho_0_bound1}
        C_1\leq \rho_g(t,x)\leq C_2
    \end{align}
    for some constants $C_1$ and $C_2$ when $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\leq 1$.\\
    
    \noindent \textbf{(b)} When $|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}>1$, then we directly use Lemma~\ref{lem:gamma_pert} for $|x_h| +\frac{z}{t} + v_z\geq 1$ to bound
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(\left||x_h|-|v_h|t\right|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z \\
        &\leq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z,
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left((|x_h|+|v_h|t)+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z \\
        &\geq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z.
    \end{align*}
    From (1) and (3) in Lemma~\ref{lem:beta_pert}, we can check that
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\right)^\beta\leq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} + \frac{8\beta t}{1+\beta},
        \intertext{and}
        \left(\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\right)^\beta\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} -\frac{4\beta t}{1+\beta}
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq \beta\leq 2$. Using Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} for $\delta_1 = 0$ and $\delta_2 = -m$, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:rho_upper_v_h}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z\\
            &\leq C\int_0^\infty \left(\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z\\
            &=C\int_{|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}}^\infty \left(v_z^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    We again apply Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} for $\delta_1 = 0$ and $\delta_2 = \frac{n-1-\beta-m}{1+\beta}$ to bound the final integral by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}}^\infty \left(v_z^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z\leq C_2\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{n-\beta-m}{1+\beta}}e^{-\alpha (|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}.
    \end{align*}
    For the same reason, we can take a lower bound of the integral by
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z\\
        &\geq C_1\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{n-\beta-m}{1+\beta}}e^{-\alpha (|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C_1$ and $C_2$. It proves
    \begin{align}\label{eq:rho_0_bound2}
        C_1\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\leq \rho_g(t,x)\leq C_2\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
    \end{align}
    when $|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\geq 1$. (As $\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}\leq 1$, it is safe to add $1$ to the exponent $(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}$ like above.)
    
    Combining \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound1} and \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2}, we conclude that \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2} holds for whole $0< t\leq \frac{1}{4}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$ for some appropriate constants $C_1$ and $C_2$. Note that the left and right side in \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2} converge to $0$ as $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\rightarrow \infty$. For the later use, we also note that \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2} holds for $m\leq 0$ as Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} still true for the $m\leq 0$ case.\\
    
    \noindent \textbullet{} \textit{$u_g$ computation.}
    
    We again take the change of variable $v_z\mapsto v_z+\frac{z}{t}$ to write
    \begin{align*}
        \rho_g\left(u_g-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)(t,x) &= e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right) g_0(x_h-v_ht, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &=e^{-t}\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} v \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    We take the upper bound of the integral by
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} |v|\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\leq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} (|v_h| + v_z)\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    For $|v_h| \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}$, we can use the results for $\rho_g$ in \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2} for $m\mapsto m-1$ and obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m+1}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
            &\leq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+1-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C$.
    
    For $v_z \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}$, we first bound the $v_h$-integral using \eqref{eq:rho_upper_v_h}. Then,
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty v_z\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht|+\frac{z}{t}+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \,dv_hdv_z\\
        &\leq C\int_0^\infty v_z\left(\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z\\
        &= C\int_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}}^\infty \left(v_z-|x_h|-\frac{z}{t}\right)\left(v_z^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    Employing Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} for $(\delta_1,\delta_2) = (1, \frac{n-1-\beta-m}{1+\beta})$ to the final $v_z$-integral, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}}^\infty \left(v_z-|x_h|-\frac{z}{t}\right)\left(v_z^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}  + 1\right)^{n-1-\beta-m}e^{-\alpha v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_z\leq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{n-\beta-m+1}{1+\beta}}e^{-\alpha (|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}.
    \end{align*}
    Combining the two results and dividing it by $\rho_g$ in \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2}, we get a constant $C_3$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:u_0_bound}
        \left|u_g - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|(t,x)\leq C_3\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}
    \end{align}
    for $0<t\leq \frac{1}{4}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$.\\
    
    \noindent \textbullet{} \textit{$T_g$ computation.}
    
    Taking the change of variable $v_z\mapsto v_z+\frac{z}{t}$, we write
    \begin{align*}
        n\rho_gT_g(t,x)&= e^{-t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}|v-u_g(t,x)|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (|x_h-v_ht| + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &=e^{-t}\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (|x_h-v_ht| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    If $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\leq 2$, by \eqref{eq:u_0_bound}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left|u_g - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2\leq 3^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}} C^2_3.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, the integral is well-bounded below and above by constants as in the $\rho_g$ and $\rho_g\left(u_g-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)$ computations, so we assume $|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\geq 2$. Applying Lemma~\ref{lem:gamma_pert} again, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\leq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z,\\
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + v_z + \frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\geq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    
    We first consider the upper bound of the integral. Decomposing the square term, we write
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\leq 2\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left(\left(|v_h|^2 + |v_z|^2\right) +\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)^2\right)\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    Iteratively applying Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} as in the $u_g$ computation, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left(|v_h|^2 + |v_z|^2\right)\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\leq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+2-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C$. Also, from \eqref{eq:u_0_bound}, we take the upper bound of the integral of the second term by
    \begin{align*}
        &\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)^2\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} - \frac{4t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\leq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+2-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}.
    \end{align*}
    Combining the two upper bounds, we obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:T_g_upper_1}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
            &\leq C'_5\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+2-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for a constant $C'_5$.
    
    For the lower bound of the integral, we need additional techniques to obtain the same asymptotic behavior as the upper bound. Our $v_h$ integration for the lower bound is on the region $|v_h|\geq R$, where
    \begin{align*}
        R=\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}.
    \end{align*}
    For fixed $t$ and $x$, $u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}$ is a fixed vector in $\mathbb{R}^n$. If we measure the distance of each point on the sphere $|v_h| = R$ from the $\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)_h = u_{g,h}(t,x)$, where $u_{g,h}$ is the horizontal component of $u_g$, the hemisphere opposite to $u_{g,h}(t,x)$ should have a distance not less than $R$. It implies that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:set_inclusion}
        \begin{split}
            &\left\{v_h:|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}, v_h\cdot u_{g,h}\leq 0\right\}\\
            &\subset \left\{v_h:|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}\right\}\cap \left\{v_h:\left|v_h -u_{g,h}\right|\geq\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\right\}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    
    We use this idea to compute a lower bound for the integral. We first take the horizontal component of the square term and write
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\\
        &\geq \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left|v_h -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)_h\right|^2 \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\\
        &\qquad \times \mathbf{1}_{\left|v_h -\left(u_g(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)_h\right|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\geq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\\
        &\qquad \times \mathbf{1}_{\left|v_h -u_{g,h}\right|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    By the set inclusion \eqref{eq:set_inclusion} and the $\rho_g$ computation \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{\left|v_h -u_{g,h}\right|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\geq \frac{1}{2}\int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}} + \frac{2t}{1+\beta}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + v_z\right)^{\frac{1-\beta}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h dv_z\\
        &\geq \frac{C_1}{2}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}.
    \end{align*}
    
    Finally, we assemble the lower bound results and combine with the upper bound result \eqref{eq:T_g_upper_1}. Then, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        C'_4\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+2-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\leq n\rho_gT_g(t,x)\leq C'_5 \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{(n+2-m)-\beta}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C'_4$ and $C'_5$. Dividing it by $\rho_g$ in \eqref{eq:rho_0_bound2}, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        C_4\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\leq T_g(t,x)\leq C_5 \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C_4$ and $C_5$ when $0<t\leq \frac{1}{4}$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$.\\
    
    \noindent \textbullet{} {\bf Finiteness of the boundary data in the exponential norm:}
    
    Now, we will show that the boundary data is finite in the exponentially weighted norm. Multiplying the exponential weight $w^h_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}$ to $e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_h t_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \langle v\rangle^\delta e^{-t_b}e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}} = e^{-t}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}.
    \end{align*}
    We first estimate the $L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+,L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n))$ norm. We initially consider $1\leq p<\infty$ since the case $p=\infty$ easily follows by a similar argument. The cutoff condition implies that $\frac{z}{t}\leq v_z\leq |v_h|^{1+\beta}$. Consequently,
    \begin{align*}
        \langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\leq \langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{z}{t}\leq v_z\leq |v_h|^{1+\beta}}.
    \end{align*}
    We take $v_z$-integral to obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:LqxLpv_0}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{p\delta} \langle v_h\rangle^{-pm}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{z}{t}\leq v_z\leq |v_h|^{1+\beta}}\,dv_z\\
            &=\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\int_{\frac{z}{t}}^{|v_h|^{1+\beta}}\left\langle v\right\rangle^{p\delta} \,dv_z\\
            &\leq C\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm + (1+\beta)(p\delta + 1)}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    
    It remains to estimate the $v_h$-integral on the the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:LqxLpv_0}. We first consider the case $|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\geq 1+t^2$. If
    \begin{align*}
        |v_h|\leq \frac{1}{2}(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},
    \end{align*}
    then we have
    \begin{align*}
        |x_h-v_ht| + \frac{z}{t}&\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\right) - |v_h|t\\
        &\geq \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\right) - \frac{1}{2}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
        &\geq \frac{1}{2}(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})\\
        &\geq |v_h|^{1+\beta}.
    \end{align*}
    It means that $|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}$ implies $|v_h|\geq \frac{1}{2}(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}$. Using this fact, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\leq \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \frac{1}{2}(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}.
    \end{align*}
    So, we bound the remaining $v_h$-integral by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:LqxLpv_1}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm + (1+\beta)(p\delta + 1)}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}} \mathbf{1}_{|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\geq 1 + t^2}\,dv_h\\
            &\leq \mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\geq 1 + t^2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \langle v_h\rangle^{-pm + (1+\beta)(p\delta + 1)} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \frac{1}{2}(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\,dv_h\\
            &\leq C\left\langle |x_h| + \frac{z}{t} \right\rangle^{p\delta +\frac{n+\beta-pm}{1+\beta}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\geq 1 + t^2}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    when $p\delta +\frac{n+\beta-pm}{1+\beta}<0$.
    
    If $0\leq |x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2$, then by the direct computation, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:LqxLpv_2}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm + (1+\beta)(p\delta + 1)}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_h t|+\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2}\, dv_h\\
            &\leq \mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm + (1+\beta)(p\delta + 1)}\, dv_h\\
            &\leq C\mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for some constant $C$ when $p\delta +\frac{n+\beta-pm}{1+\beta}<0$. Combining \eqref{eq:LqxLpv_0}, \eqref{eq:LqxLpv_1}, and \eqref{eq:LqxLpv_2}, we get
    \begin{align*}
        &\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}\left(\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right)^p\,dv\right)^{1/p}\\
        &\leq C\left(\left\langle |x_h| + \frac{z}{t} \right\rangle^{\delta+\frac{(n+\beta)/p-m}{1+\beta}}\mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\geq 1 + t^2} + \mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2}\right)
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq p< \infty$.
    
    Next, suppose $1\leq q<\infty$. If we choose $m$ satisfying $\delta+\frac{(n+\beta)/p-m}{1+\beta}<-\frac{n}{q}$,
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^\infty \left\langle |x_h| + \frac{z}{t}\right\rangle^{q\left(\delta+\frac{(n+\beta)/p-m}{1+\beta}\right)}\,dz dx_h&=t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^\infty \left\langle |x_h| + z\right\rangle^{q\left(\delta+\frac{(n+\beta)/p-m}{1+\beta}\right)}\,dz dx_h\\
        &<Ct
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C$. Also,
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}_{|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}\leq 1 + t^2}\,dzdx_h\leq C(1+t^2)^n t.
    \end{align*}
    It shows that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:LqxLpv_3}
        \left\|e^{-t_b}\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right\|_{L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+, L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n))}\leq C(1+t^2)^{n/q} t^{1/q}e^{-t}
    \end{align}
    when
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{n+\beta}{p}+\frac{(n+q\delta)(1+\beta)}{q}<m
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq q<\infty$. Note that the $e^{-t}$ term is from the definition of the $g$ in \eqref{eq:boundary_data}. When $p=\infty$ or $q=\infty$, we can repeat a similar calculation and conclude that the same results still hold with the convention $1/\infty = 0$. Therefore, for any fixed $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$, the boundary data $g$ satisfies \eqref{eq:LqxLpv_3} when $m$ meets the condition \eqref{eq:m_cond1}.\\
    
    Next, we do the $x$ integral first to compute 
    \begin{align}
        &\left\|e^{-t_b}\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\notag\\
        &=e^{-t}\left\|v_z^{1/q}e^\tau\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(\tau,x_h,v)\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^{n}_+))}\notag\\
        &=e^{-t}\left\|v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left||x_h-v_h\tau| + v_z\right|^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{\tau,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^{n}_+))}\notag\\
        &=t^{1/q}e^{-t}\left\|v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left||x_h| + v_z\right|^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{x_h}(\partial \mathbb{R}^{n}_+))}.\label{eq:g_LpvLqx_form}
    \end{align}
    For the first equality, we used Lemma~\ref{lem:trace} for the norm equivalence. For $1\leq q<\infty$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \left(v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left||x_h| + v_z\right|^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right)^q\,dx_h \right)^{1/q}\\
        &=v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left||x_h| + v_z\right|^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\,dx_h\right)^{1/q}\\
        &=\left(\frac{|\mathbb{S}^{n-2}|}{n-1}\right)^{1/q}v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\left(\max\{|v_h|^{1+\beta}-v_z, 0\}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{q}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}.
    \end{align*}
    When $1\leq p<\infty$, we also have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^{|v_h|^{1+\beta}} v_z^{p/q}\langle v\rangle^{p\delta}\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm}\left(|v_h|^{1+\beta}-v_z\right)^{\frac{p(n-1)}{q}}\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^{|v_h|^{1+\beta}} v_z^{p/q}\langle v\rangle^{p\delta}\langle v_h\rangle^{-pm}|v_h|^{\frac{p(n-1)(1+\beta)}{q}}\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\langle v_h\rangle^{(p/q + p\delta + 1)(1+\beta)-pm+\frac{p(n-1)(1+\beta)}{q}}\,dv_h.
    \end{align*}
    It is integrable if
    \begin{align*}
        (\delta+1/p)(1+\beta)-m+\frac{n(1+\beta)}{q}<-\frac{n-1}{p}
    \end{align*}
    Therefore,
    \begin{align*}
        &\left\|e^{-t_b}\langle v\rangle^\delta e^{\alpha|v_h|^\beta}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\leq Ct^{1/q}e^{-t}
    \end{align*}
    if the constants satisfy \eqref{eq:m_cond1} for $1\leq p,q<\infty$. One can do the same calculation for $p=\infty$ or $q=\infty$ and obtain the identical result under the convention $1/\infty = 0$.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:M[f]_integral}
    Let $n\geq2$, $m_1\in\mathbb{R}$, $m_2\geq 0$, $\alpha\geq 0$, and $1\leq\beta\leq 2$. Assume that there exist positive constants $C_i$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:macro_bound}
        \begin{split}
            &C_1\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{m_1}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\leq \rho(t,x)\leq C_2\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{m_1}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}},\\
            &\left|u(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq C_3 \left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\\
            &C_4\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\leq T(t,x)\leq C_5\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $0< t\leq \frac{1}{4}$. Then, there exists a constant $C>0$, depending on $C_i$, $n$, $m_1$, $m_2$, $\alpha$, and $\beta$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:M[f]_integral_res}
        \begin{split}
            &\sup_{0<\tau\leq t}\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}\left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^{m_2} \frac{\rho(\tau, x-v(t-\tau))}{(2\pi T(\tau, x-v(t-\tau)))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v-u(\tau, x-v(t-\tau))|^2}{2T(\tau, x-v(t-\tau))}\right)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)>0}\,dv\\
            &\leq C\left(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{\frac{m_1+m_2}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $0<t\leq \frac{1}{4}$.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Since the proof is lengthy and technically involved, we first provide a brief overview of the main ideas. The first step is to simplify the complicated local Maxwellian integrand using the macroscopic field conditions \eqref{eq:macro_bound}. For example, because of the boundary effect, we separate the velocity integration into the tangential $v_h$-integration and normal $v_z$-integration and treat the two integrals differently. Through a chain of change of variables, we will observe that the term $\frac{z}{t}$ naturally appears in the integral in the $v_z$-integral as in the proof of Proposition ~\ref{prop:boundary_int}.
    
    We then derive explicit and sharp bounds for the simplified integrals. Since the transport term shifts the spatial argument from $x$ to $x-v(t-\tau)$, it is natural to partition the velocity-integration domains according to the relevant spatial scales. More precisely, we decompose the $v_z$-integration domain into $A_1$ and $A_2$, and the $v_h$-integration domain into $B_1$ and $B_2$, as defined below, and estimate the integrals over the subdomains. At first, we use Lemma~\ref{lem:concaten} to extract the same exponential decay factor as that appearing in the bound for $\rho$ in \eqref{eq:macro_bound}. We then bound the remaining integral by the desired polynomial term, thereby obtaining \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_res}.
    
    We start the proof by simplifying the integral. Let $f(t,x,v)$ be a function whose macroscopic fields satisfy \eqref{eq:macro_bound}. Then the associated local Maxwellian can be written as follows:
    \begin{align*}
        \mathcal{M}(f)(t, x, v) = \frac{\rho(t, x)}{(2\pi T(t, x))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v-u(t, x)|^2}{2T(t, x)}\right).
    \end{align*}
    By change of variable $v_z\mapsto v_z + \frac{z}{t}$, we first have
    \begin{align*}
        &\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^{m_2} \mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)>0}\,dv\\
        &=\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}|v|^{m_2}\mathcal{M}(f)\left(\tau, x-\left(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)(t-\tau), v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\frac{\tau}{t}z - v_z(t-\tau) >0}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    We expand the local Maxwellian by
    \begin{align*}
        &\mathcal{M}(f)\left(\tau, x-\left(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)(t-\tau), v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\\
        &=\frac{\rho(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}{(2\pi T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})-u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))|^2}{2T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\right).
    \end{align*}
    The $z$-component of the velocity part in the exponent is rearranged into
    \begin{align*}
        &v_z + \frac{z}{t}-u_z(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))\\
        &= v_z + \frac{z}{t} - \left(u_z(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z+\frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\right) - \frac{z-(v_z+\frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\\
        &=\frac{t}{\tau}v_z - \left(u_z(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z+\frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\right).
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, we can rewrite the exponent to
    \begin{align*}
        \left(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)-u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) = \left(v_h,\frac{t}{\tau}v_z\right) - \left(u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z+\frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\hat{z}\right).
    \end{align*}
    Furthermore, from \eqref{eq:macro_bound}, the second term is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        \left|u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z+\frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\hat{z}\right|\leq \frac{C_3}{\sqrt{C_4}}\sqrt{T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}.
    \end{align*}
    Also, using the simple inequality $(x-y)^2\geq \frac{1}{2}x^2 - y^2$ for $x,y\geq 0$, we can bound the exponent in exponential term by
    \begin{align*}
        &-\frac{\left|(v_h, \frac{t}{\tau}v_z) - \left(u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z + \frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2}{2T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\\
        &\leq -\frac{\frac{1}{2}|(v_h, \frac{t}{\tau}v_z)|^2 - |u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau)) - \frac{z-(v_z + \frac{z}{t})(t-\tau)}{\tau}\hat{z}|^2}{2T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\\
        &\leq \frac{C^2_3}{2C_4} - \frac{\frac{1}{2}|(v_h, \frac{t}{\tau}v_z)|^2}{2T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}.
    \end{align*}
    These bounds yield
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v|^{m_2}\frac{\rho(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}{T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})-u(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))|^2}{2T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\frac{\tau}{t}z - v_z(t-\tau) >0}\,dv\\
        &\leq e^{\frac{C_3^2}{2C_4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v|^{m_2}\frac{\rho(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}{T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|(v_h, \frac{t}{\tau}v_z)|^2}{4T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\frac{\tau}{t}z - v_z(t-\tau) >0}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    If we assume $\tau = t$, then the integral is the same as the plain Gaussian integral
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v|^{m_2}\frac{\rho(t, x)}{T(t, x)^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v|^2}{4T(t, x)}\right)\mathbf{1}_{z>0}\,dv,
    \end{align*}
    and we directly obtain the target bound \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_res}. Therefore, we assume $0<\tau<t$ from now on and write the cutoff function $\mathbf{1}_{\frac{\tau}{t}z - v_z(t-\tau) >0} = \mathbf{1}_{v_z < \frac{\tau z}{t(t-\tau)}}$.
    
    We split $|v|^{m_2} \leq C(|v_h|^{m_2} + |v_z|^{m_2})$ for some constant $C$ for given $m_2\geq 0$. Replacing $\rho$ and $T$ by \eqref{eq:macro_bound}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v|^{m_2}\frac{\rho(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}{T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|(v_h, \frac{t}{\tau}v_z)|^2}{4T(\tau, x-(v+\frac{z}{t}\hat{z})(t-\tau))}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{\tau z}{t(t-\tau)}}\,dv\\
        &\leq C\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} (|v_h|^{m_2} + |v_z|^{m_2})\frac{(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - \frac{(t-\tau)v_z}{\tau} + 1)^{\frac{m_1}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha (|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - \frac{(t-\tau)v_z}{\tau} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}}{(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - \frac{(t-\tau)v_z}{\tau} + 1)^{\frac{n}{1+\beta}}}\\
        &\qquad \times \exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + |\frac{t}{\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - \frac{(t-\tau)v_z}{\tau} + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{\tau z}{t(t-\tau)}}\,dv\\
        &= C\frac{t}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v_h|^{m_2} + \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\right) \frac{e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}}{(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{n-m_1}{1+\beta}}}\\
        &\qquad \times \exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + |\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    In the last equality, we used a change of variable $v_z\mapsto \frac{\tau}{t-\tau}v_z$. For later use, we generalize the notation and write the desired bound by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:M[f]_integral_form1}
        \begin{split}
            &\frac{t}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v_h|^{m_{2,1}}\left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_{2,2}} \frac{e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}}{(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{n-m_1}{1+\beta}}}\\
            &\qquad \times \exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + |\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
            &\leq C(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{m_1+m_{2,1} + m_{2,2}}{1+\beta}} e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t}+1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for a constant depending on $C_i$, $n$, $m_1$, $m_2$, $\alpha$, and $\beta$. The original integral can be recovered by choosing $(m_{2,1},m_{2,2}) = (m_2, 0)$ and $(m_{2,1},m_{2,2}) = (0, m_2)$.

    To bound this integral, we separate the $v_z$ and $v_h$-integrals and decompose each integration domain by
    \begin{align*}
        A_1&:=\left\{v_z\in\mathbb R:-2\left(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)|+\frac{z}{t}+1\right)\leq v_z<\frac{z}{t}\right\},\\
        A_2&:=\left\{v_z\in\mathbb R:v_z\leq-2\left(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)|+\frac{z}{t}+1\right) \right\}
    \end{align*}
    for the $v_z$ domain $(-\infty, \frac{z}{t}]$ and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:v_h_division}
        \begin{aligned}
            B_1&:=\left\{v_h\in\mathbb R^{n-1}:|v_h|(t-\tau)\leq 2\left(|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}+1\right)\right\},\\
            B_2&:=\left\{v_h\in\mathbb R^{n-1}:|v_h|(t-\tau)\geq 2\left(|x_h|+\frac{z}{t}+1\right)\right\}
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    for the $v_h$ domain $\mathbb{R}^{n-1}$.
    
    We first consider the domain $A_1\times \mathbb{R}^{n-1}$. We will perform $v_z$-integral on $A_1$ and then $v_h$-integral on $B_1$ and $B_2$ and derive the desired bound \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_res}. Next, we prove that the integral on $A_2\times \mathbb{R}^{n-1}$ is a negligible term compared to the integral on $A_1\times\mathbb{R}^{n-1}$ and conclude the result.\\
    
    \noindent\textbf{Step 1: Integral over $A_1\times\mathbb{R}^{n-1}$.} 
    
    Now, we consider the domain $A_1\times \mathbb{R}^{n-1}$, so
    \begin{align*}
        -2\left(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)|+\frac{z}{t}+1\right)< v_z\leq\frac{z}{t}.
    \end{align*}
    For pretreatment, we extract $e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}$ term from the exponential parts in \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_form1}. We first split the exponent function in the local Maxwellian for the $v_z$ direction into half and multiply with the exponential function in the density $\rho$. Then, by Lemma \ref{lem:concaten},
    \begin{align}\label{eq:concaten_example1}
        \begin{split}
            &e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\\
            &=e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\right)^2\\
            &\leq Ce^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} +\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}-1\right)|v_z| + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\\
            &\leq Ce^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for a constant $C$ depending on $C_5$ and $\beta$. We can repeat this for the $v_h$ variable. For the $v_h$ part of the local Maxwellian in \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_form1}, as $v_z\geq -2\left(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1\right)$, we write
    \begin{align*}
        \exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2}{4C_5(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\leq \exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2}{4C_5\left(3(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right).
    \end{align*}
    We now split this local Maxwellian function and apply Lemma \ref{lem:concaten}, as in \eqref{eq:concaten_example1}, to obtain
    \begin{align*}
        &e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2}{4C_5\left(3(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\\
        &\leq C e^{-\alpha(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + |v_h| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2}{8C_5\left(3(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)\\
        &\leq C e^{-\alpha(|x_h| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2}{8C_5\left(3(|x_h-v_h(t-\tau)| + \frac{z}{t} + 1)\right)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}}\right)
    \end{align*}
    as $0\leq t-\tau\leq \frac{1}{4}$. Finally, the integral \eqref{eq:M[f]_integral_form1} on $A_1\times\mathbb{R}^{n-1}$ is bounded by the integral
