    \end{align*}
    If we choose $c$ from
    \begin{align*}
        c\in\left[\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\}, \min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\}\right],
    \end{align*}
    we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\alpha'c -\frac{4}{3}\frac{c^2}{C_T}\right)(|x_h| + 1)\geq C(|x_h| + 1)
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C>0$ depending on $\min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\}$. It shows that the growth term is much bigger than the decay term in the given range of $c$. Therefore, there again exist sufficiently large $M>0$ and small $\epsilon>0$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        e^{\alpha' |v_h|}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z\geq v_z(t-\tau)}\geq e^{\epsilon (|x_h| + 1)}
    \end{align*}
    for $|x_h|\geq M$ under the choices of \eqref{eq:Lq_xLp_v_choice} and
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\} (|x_h| + 1)\leq |v_h|\leq \min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\} (|x_h| + 1).
    \end{align*}
    
    Finally, for $1\leq p<\infty$, we obtain the lower bound of $L^p_v$ norm of $f(t,x,v)$ as
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(e^{\alpha'|v_h|^\beta}f(t,x,v)\right)^p\,dv\\
        &\geq e^{-pt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(\int_0^t e^{\alpha'|v_h|^\beta}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau,x-v(t-\tau),v)\mathbf{1}_{z - v_z(t-\tau)>0}\,d\tau\right)^p\,dv\\
        &\geq e^{-pt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^{\frac{z}{4t}} \left(\int_{\frac{1}{2}\min\{t, t_0\}}^{\min\{t, t_0\}} e^{\alpha'|v_h|^\beta}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau,x-v(t-\tau),v)\mathbf{1}_{v_h\in A}\,d\tau\right)^p\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\geq e^{-pt}e^{p\epsilon (|x_h| + 1)^{\frac{2\beta}{1+\beta}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_0^{\frac{z}{4t}} \left(\int_{\frac{1}{2}\min\{t, t_0\}}^{\min\{t, t_0\}} \mathbf{1}_{v_h\in A}\,d\tau\right)^p\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\geq C(\min\{t, t_0\})^p \frac{z}{4t}e^{-pt}e^{p\epsilon (|x_h| + 1)^{\frac{2\beta}{1+\beta}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\mathbf{1}_{v_h\in A}\,dv_h
    \end{align*}
    for $\frac{\min\{t, t_0\}}{2}\leq z\leq \min\{t, t_0\}$, where $A$ is the set given by
    \begin{align*}
        A:=\begin{dcases}
            \left\{v_h:\frac{1}{8\max\{t, 1\}}(|x_h|+1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\leq |v_h|\leq \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}(|x_h|+1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\right\} & 1<\beta\leq 2,\\
            \left\{v_h:\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\} (|x_h| + 1)\leq |v_h|\leq \min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\} (|x_h| + 1)\right\}& \beta = 1.
        \end{dcases}
    \end{align*}
    The integral of the set is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\mathbf{1}_{v_h\in A}\,dv_h\geq C\left(\min\left\{\frac{3\alpha' C_T}{8}, \frac{1}{4\max\{t, 1\}}\right\} (|x_h| + 1)^{\frac{2}{1+\beta}}\right)^{n-1}.
    \end{align*}
    Due to the exponential factor $e^{\epsilon (|x_h| + 1)^{\frac{2\beta}{1+\beta}}}$, we obtain the exponential ill-posedness \eqref{eq:Lq_xLp_v_res} for any $1\leq q\leq \infty$. If $p=\infty$, since
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|e^{\alpha'|v_h|^\beta}f(t,x,v)\right\|_{L^\infty_v(\mathbb{R}^n)}\geq \frac{\min\{t, t_0\}}{2} e^{-t}e^{\epsilon (|x_h| + 1)^{\frac{2\beta}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{\min\{t, t_0\}}{2}\leq z\leq \min\{t, t_0\}},
    \end{align*}
    we again get \eqref{eq:Lq_xLp_v_res} for $1\leq q\leq \infty$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
For given constants, we choose the boundary data $g(t,x_h,v)$ of the form \eqref{eq:boundary_data} satisfying the condition \eqref{eq:m_cond1}; as noted in the introduction of Section~\ref{sec:Macro_quan}, we can rescale $g$ by $\eta g$ for any sufficiently small $\eta>0$ to meet the condition \eqref{3_thm:main_BGK_1_cond}. From Proposition \ref{prop:boundary_int}, we have the boundedness of the boundary data under the exponential weight. To make the weighted norm of the boundary data arbitrarily small, we choose the boundary data as $\epsilon g$ for any $\epsilon>0$ by the homogeneity of the BGK model equation; we refer to the argument right after \eqref{eq:boundary_data}. Using Theorem \ref{thm:BGK_sol}, we construct a unique solution $f$ of the BGK model in $\Omega_{\frac{1}{1+\beta},t_0}$ such that the macroscopic fields of $f$ satisfy \eqref{eq:X_T_A3} pointwise. For any BGK solution $\tilde{f}$ such that $f(t,x,v) = \tilde{f}(t,x,v)$ for $0\leq t\leq t_0$, we have the desired exponential-norm blow-up by Proposition \ref{prop:Ill_posed}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:LPY_lem}
    Let $n\geq 2$, $\alpha>0$, $\max\left\{0, \frac{2}{n-1}\kappa, \frac{2}{3}(1+\kappa)\right\}<\beta\leq 2$, and $\delta> n$. Then, for functions $f$ and $h$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}Q^+(f,h)(x,v)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\leq C_{gain,1}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}h\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C_{gain,1}$ depending on $\alpha$, $\beta$, $\delta$, $\kappa$, and the dimension $n$.
    For general $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$, we further assume $\delta>n+1$. If $n=2$, we also impose $1+\kappa\leq \beta$. Then, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}Q^+(f,f)(x,v)\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\leq C_{gain,2}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C_{gain,2}$ depending on $\alpha$, $\beta$, $\delta$, $\kappa$, and $n$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
The original results in \cite{LPY2024} were established in the whole space setting $\mathbb{R}^n$. To extend these results to the half space, we just extend the function $f$ on $\mathbb{R}^n_+$ by
    \begin{align*}
        \tilde{f}(x,v) = \begin{cases}
            f(x,v) & z\geq 0,\\
            0 & z<0
        \end{cases}
    \end{align*}
    and apply Lemma 5.5 and 5.10 in \cite{LPY2024} for $\tilde{f}$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:boltzmann_existence}
    Let $n\geq 2$, $\alpha>0$, $\max\left\{0, \frac{2}{n-1}\kappa, \frac{2}{3}(1+\kappa)\right\}<\beta\leq 2$, and $\delta> n+1$. If the boundary data on $\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+$ satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L_{t,x_h,v}^\infty([0, \infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}<\infty,
    \end{align*}
    then there exists a unique solution of the Boltzmann equation $f(t,x,v)$ in $L_{loc, t}^\infty([0,t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}))$ which satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\leq \frac{1}{\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}g(t,x_h,v)\|^{-1}_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0, \infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)} - C_{gain,1}t},
    \end{align*}
    where $C_{gain,1}$ is given in Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem}. Here, $t_0$ is given by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:t_0_def}
        t_0 = \left(C_{gain,1}\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0, \infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\right)^{-1}.
    \end{align}
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Define a solution map $\mathcal{T}$ by
    \begin{align*}
        \mathcal{T}f(t,x,v) &= e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^t e^{-\int_\tau^t L(f)(s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} Q^+(f,f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    We claim that if
    \begin{align}\label{eq:apriori_esti}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\leq \frac{1}{\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|^{-1}_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)} - C_{gain,1}t},
    \end{align}
    then $\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}\mathcal{T}f(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}$ also follows the same bound along time, and so the solution map
    \begin{align*}
        \mathcal{T}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}
    \end{align*}
    is well-defined, where $\mathcal{A}$ is the collection of functions in $L^\infty_{loc, t}([0, t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$ satisfying \eqref{eq:apriori_esti} and $f\geq 0$. Indeed, if $f\in \mathcal{A}$ for some $T'>0$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem} and \ref{lem:trace}, we have
    
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}\mathcal{T}f(t,x,v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}&\leq \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} Q^+(f,f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\,d\tau\\
        &\leq \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)} + C_{gain,1}\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} f(\tau,x,v)\|^2_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\,d\tau
    \end{align*}
    for each $0\leq t\leq T'$. Therefore, if $f$ satisfies \eqref{eq:apriori_esti}, then
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}\mathcal{T}f(t,x,v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}&\leq \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\\
        &\quad + C_{gain,1}\int_0^t \frac{1}{(\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|^{-1}_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)} - C_{gain,1}\tau)^2}\,d\tau\\
        &=\frac{1}{\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|^{-1}_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)} - C_{gain,1}t}.
    \end{align*}
    
    With this \textit{a priori} estimate, we can prove the existence of a fixed point of $\mathcal{T}$ in $\mathcal{A}$ satisfying \eqref{eq:apriori_esti} for small enough $T_1$ by checking the contraction property of $\mathcal{T}$. We estimate difference $|\mathcal{T}f_1-\mathcal{T}f_2|$ as follows:
    \begin{align}
        &|\mathcal{T}f_1-\mathcal{T}f_2|(t,x,v)\notag\\
        &\leq \left|e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_1](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau} - e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_2](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}\right|g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\label{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_1}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^t \left|e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_1](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} - e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds}\right| Q^+(f_1,f_1)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\label{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_2}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^t e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} \left|Q^+(f_1,f_1) - Q^+(f_2,f_2)\right|(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\label{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_3}.
    \end{align}
    We split the proof into the cases $\kappa>0$ and $\kappa\leq 0$.\\
    
    \noindent \textit{(i) The case $\kappa>0$.}
    When $\kappa>0$, we multiply $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)$ on the both sides. Since $|e^{-x}-e^{-y}|\leq |x-y|$ for $x,y\geq 0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\left|\left(e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_1](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} - e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds}\right)\right|\\
        &\leq w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\int_\tau^t \left|L[f_1]-L[f_2]\right|(s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds\\
        &\leq w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)(1+|v|^2)^{\kappa/2}\int_\tau^t \iint_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}^n}(1+|v_*|^2)^{\kappa/2}b(\cos\theta)(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v_*))^{-1}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(s)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,dv_*d\omega ds\\
        &\leq C w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v)\int_0^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds
    \end{align*}
    for $0\leq \tau\leq t$. In the middle, we used
    \begin{align}\label{eq:L_bound}
        \begin{split}
            \left|L[f_1]-L[f_2]\right|(v) &\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|v-v_*|^\kappa |\cos\theta| |f_1-f_2|(v_*)\,dv_* d\omega\\
            &\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}(1+|v|^2)^{\kappa/2}(1+|v_*|^2)^{\kappa/2} |\cos\theta| |f_1-f_2|(v_*)\,dv_* d\omega\\
            &\leq C(1+|v|^2)^{\kappa/2} \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)\|_{L^\infty_v}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}} |\cos\theta|(1+|v_*|^2)^{\kappa-\delta/2}e^{-\alpha |v_*|^\beta}\,dv_*d\omega\\
            &\leq C(1+|v|^2)^{\kappa/2} \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)\|_{L^\infty_v}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Thus, combining this bound with Lemma~\ref{lem:trace}, we obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:diff_esti2}
        \begin{split}
            &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\left|e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_1](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau} - e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_2](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}\right| g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\\
            &\leq C \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\int_0^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(\tau)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for \eqref{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_1}. For \eqref{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_2}, we use Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem} to obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:diff_esti3}
        \begin{split}
            &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\int_0^t \left|e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_1](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} - e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds}\right| Q^+(f_1,f_1)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
            &\leq C\int_0^t w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v) Q^+(f_1,f_1)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\int_\tau^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds \,d\tau\\
            &\leq C\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|^2_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\int_\tau^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds \,d\tau\\
            &\leq C\sup_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|^2_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\int_0^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $t\leq 1$. Finally, we decompose the gain term $Q^+$ and apply Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem} to bound \eqref{eq:Boltzmann_contraction_3} by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:diff_esti4}
        \begin{split}
            &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\int_0^t e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} \left|Q^+(f_1,f_1) - Q^+(f_2,f_2)\right|(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
            &\leq w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}(v)\int_0^t \left(\left|Q^+(f_1-f_2,f_1)\right| + \left|Q^+(f_2,f_2-f_1)\right|\right)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
            &\leq C\int_0^t (\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_2(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}})\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa} (f_1-f_2)(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}

    Combining \eqref{eq:diff_esti2}, \eqref{eq:diff_esti3}, and \eqref{eq:diff_esti4}, we get
    \begin{align}\label{eq:contract_kappa>0}
        \begin{split}
            &\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa}(\mathcal{T}f_1-\mathcal{T}f_2)(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\\
            &\leq C\left[\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}} + \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\left(\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}^2 + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_2(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\right)\right]\\
            &\qquad \times \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa} (f_1-f_2)(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $t\leq 1$.
    
    Now, we construct a function sequence $f^i = \mathcal{T}f^{i-1}$ with $f^0(t,x,v) = 0$. Then, $f^i$ satisfies \eqref{eq:apriori_esti} and $f^i\geq 0$ for each $i\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}$. Therefore, setting $f_1 = f^i$ and $f_2 = f^{i-1}$ in \eqref{eq:contract_kappa>0}, we get
    \begin{align*}
        &\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa}(f^{i+1}-f^i)(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\\
        &\leq C\left[\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}} + \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\left(\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f^i(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}^2 + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f^i(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f^{i-1}(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\right)\right]\\
        &\qquad \times \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa} (f^i-f^{i-1})(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    Choosing small enough $T_1\leq 1$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa}(f^{i+1}-f^i)(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\leq \frac{1}{2}\sup_{0\leq \tau\leq T_1}\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa} (f^i-f^{i-1})(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}
    \end{align*}
    for $0\leq t\leq T_1$. Therefore, there exists a fixed point $f(t,x,v)$ of $\mathcal{T}$ in $L^\infty_t([0, T_1], L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa}))$, which is a solution of the Boltzmann equation. Since $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f^i(t,x,v)\rightarrow w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)$ in a.e. $(t,x,v)$, and $f^i\in \mathcal{A}$ for all $i$, we conclude that $f\in L^\infty_t([0, T_1], L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$ satisfying \eqref{eq:apriori_esti} and $f\geq 0$.
    
    To prove uniqueness of the solution in $L^\infty_t([0, T_1], L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$, suppose there is another solution $h(t,x,v)$ in $L^\infty_t([0, T_1], L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$. Plugging $f_1 = f$ and $f_2 = h$ into \eqref{eq:contract_kappa>0}, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        &\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa}(f-h)(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\\
        &\leq C\left[\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}} + \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\left(\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}^2 + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}h(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\right)\right]\\
        &\qquad \times \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa} (f-h)(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    for a.e. $t$. By Gr{\"o}nwall's inequality, we have $f=h$ for a.e. $(t,x,v)$, so in $L^\infty_t([0, T_1], L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$.

    To extend the solution in time, assume we have constructed the solution $f(t,x,v)$ for $t\in [0, T']$ and $\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta}f(T', x, v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}<\infty$. Treating $f(T', x, v)$ as initial data, we can extend the solution map starting from $T'$ by
    \begin{align*}
        \mathcal{T}f(t+T',x,v) &= e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L(f)(\tau+T', x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}g(t+T'-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}} \\
        &\quad + e^{-\int_0^t L(f)(\tau+T', x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}f(T', x-vt, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z<\frac{z}{t}}\\
        &\quad + \int_0^t e^{-\int_\tau^t L(f)(s+T', x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} Q^+(f,f)(\tau+T', x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    We can repeat the same computations as above and obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \begin{split}
            &\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta-\kappa}(\mathcal{T}f_1-\mathcal{T}f_2)(t+T')\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\\
            &\leq C\bigg[\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}} + \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} f(T',x,v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} \\
            &\quad + \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\left(\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau+T')\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}^2 + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_1(\tau+T')\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_2(\tau+T')\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\right)\bigg]\\
            &\qquad \times \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta-\kappa} (f_1-f_2)(\tau+T')\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
        \end{split}
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, as long as the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:apriori_esti} is finite, we can extend the time domain for the solution with uniqueness. It concludes the existence and uniqueness of the solution $f(t,x,v)$ in $L^\infty_{loc, t}([0,t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}))$ satisfying \eqref{eq:apriori_esti}.\\

    \noindent \textit{(ii) The case $\kappa\leq 0$.} In this case, we use the original weight $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v)$. The only problematic part in the previous case was to bound the difference term $|L[f_1]-L[f_2]|$, which generated $(1+|v|)^\kappa$ term from the collision kernel $B$ as in \eqref{eq:L_bound}. If $\kappa\leq 0$, however, it does not produce the $(1+|v|)^\kappa$ factor. Indeed, we write
    \begin{align*}
        \left|L[f_1]-L[f_2]\right|(v)&\leq \int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}\times \mathbb{R}^n} |v-v_*|^\kappa b(\cos\theta) |f_1-f_2|(v_*)\,d\omega dv_*\\
        &\leq C\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(f_1-f_2)\|_{L^\infty_v}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v-v_*|^\kappa (w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta})^{-1}(v_*)\,dv_*.
    \end{align*}
    Since $-n<\kappa\leq 0$, by the Hardy-Littlewood inequality, the last integral is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v-v_*|^\kappa (w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta})^{-1}(v_*)\,dv_*\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v_*|^\kappa (w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta})^{-1}(v_*)\,dv_*<\infty.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, we can modify \eqref{eq:diff_esti2} by
    \begin{align*}
        &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v)\left|e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_1](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau} - e^{-\int_{t-t_b}^t L[f_2](\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\,d\tau}\right| g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\\
        &\leq C \|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial\mathbb{R}^n_+\times\mathbb{R}^n)}\int_0^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(f_1-f_2)(\tau)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    For \eqref{eq:diff_esti3} and \eqref{eq:diff_esti4}, we use a similar computation and get
    \begin{align*}
        &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v)\int_0^t \left|e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_1](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} - e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds}\right| Q^+(f_1,f_1)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
        &\leq C\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|^2_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\int_\tau^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds \,d\tau\\
        &\leq C\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq t}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|^2_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\int_0^t \left\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(f_1-f_2)(s)\right\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,ds,
        \intertext{and}
        &w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}(v)\int_0^t e^{-\int_\tau^t L[f_2](s, x-v(t-s), v)\,ds} \left|Q^+(f_1,f_1) - Q^+(f_2,f_2)\right|(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
        &\leq C\int_0^t (\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_2(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}})\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} (f_1-f_2)(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau
    \end{align*}
    for $-n<\kappa\leq 0$ and $t\leq 1$. Collecting all the bounds, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:contract_kappa<=0}
        \begin{split}
            &\|w_{\alpha,\beta, \delta}(\mathcal{T}f_1-\mathcal{T}f_2)(t)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\\
            &\leq C\left[\|w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}} + \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0\leq \tau\leq t}\left(\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}^2 + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_1(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}} + \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f_2(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\right)\right]\\
            &\qquad \times \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} (f_1-f_2)(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}\,d\tau.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Starting from \eqref{eq:contract_kappa<=0}, we follow the remaining steps for $\kappa>0$ and establish the existence and uniqueness of the solution satisfying \eqref{eq:apriori_esti}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
In the proof, we can check that $L(f)(t,x,v)$ and $Q^+(f,f)(t,x,v)$ are in $L^1_{loc}(t,x,v)$ if $f(t,x,v)\in L^\infty_{loc,t}([0, t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}))$ as a byproduct. Therefore, by Lemma~\ref{lem:mild_sol_equiv}, the mild solution $f$ also satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        f(t,x,v) = g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}} + \int_0^t Q(f,f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau,
    \end{align*}
    which is another presentation of a mild solution.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
We can slightly modify the proof and get the result that there exists a unique solution $f(t,x,v)$ as long as $\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}f(t,x,v)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}}<\infty$ by repeatedly extending the time domain of the solution.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:boltzmann_wellposed}
    Assume $g$ satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \|v_z^{1/q}w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t_0)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}<\infty
    \end{align*}
    for $\alpha>0$, $\max\left\{0, \frac{2}{n-1}\kappa, \frac{2}{3}(1+\kappa)\right\}<\beta\leq 2$, $\delta>n+1$, and $t_0$ defined by \eqref{eq:t_0_def}. If $n=2$, we further impose a condition $1+\kappa\leq \beta$. Then, the solution of the Boltzmann equation $f(t,x,v)$ for $t\in [0, t_0)$ constructed above satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(t)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\leq \|v_z^{1/q} w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t_0)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}\exp\left(C_{gain,2}\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}^n_+)}\,d\tau\right)
    \end{align*}
    for $0\leq t<t_0$. Here, $C_{gain, 2}$ is from Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem}.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By Lemma~\ref{lem:trace}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g(t-t_b,x-vt_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}&=\|v_z^{1/q}w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}\\
        &\leq \|v_z^{1/q}w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t_0)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}.
    \end{align*}
    Employing Lemma~\ref{lem:LPY_lem}, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(t)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}&\leq \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g(t-t_b,x-vt_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))} \\
        &\quad + \int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}Q^+(f,f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\,d\tau\\
        &\leq \|v_z^{1/q} w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g(t,x_h, v)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t_0)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}\\
        &\quad + C_{gain,2}\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}^n_+)}\|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(\tau)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\,d\tau.
    \end{align*}
    By Gr{\"o}nwall's inequality, we get \textit{a priori} estimate
    \begin{align*}
        \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(t)\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_x(\mathbb{R}^n_+))}\leq \|v_z^{1/q} w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} g\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0, t_0)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}\exp\left(C_{gain,2}\int_0^t \|w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta} f(\tau)\|_{L^\infty_{x,v}(\mathbb{R}^n\times\mathbb{R}^n_+)}\,d\tau\right)
    \end{align*}
    for $0\leq t< t_0$. Finally, we apply this \textit{a priori} estimate to the iteration scheme of Proposition~\ref{prop:boltzmann_existence} to conclude the proposition.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
In some range of $\kappa$ and $\beta$, we can construct a unique solution of the Boltzmann equation with the inflow boundary data $g(t,x_h,v)$ of the form \eqref{eq:boundary_data}. Let $g$ be given by \eqref{eq:boundary_data} for some $\alpha>0$, $0<\beta\leq 2$, and $m\geq 0$ to be determined later. We choose the weight function $w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}, \delta}$ with $\delta>n + 1$. We write the multiplication of $g$ and the weight function as
    \begin{align*}
        w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}, \delta}(v)g(t,x_h,v) &= e^{-t} w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}, \delta}(v) \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (|x_h-v_h t| + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\\
        &=e^{-t}\langle v\rangle^\delta\langle v_h\rangle^{-m} e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{2} |v|^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (|x_h-v_h t| + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}.
    \end{align*}
    If $|v_h|\geq v_z$ and $|v_h|\geq 1$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:beta_pert}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\left(|v_h|^2 + v_z^2\right)^{\frac{\beta}{2(1+\beta)}} - |v_h|^\beta\leq \frac{1}{2}\left(|v_h|^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} + \frac{\beta}{2(1+\beta)}|v_h|^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}\right) - |v_h|^\beta\leq 0.
    \end{align*}
    If $|v_h|\leq v_z$, as $v_z\leq |v_h|^{1+\beta}$ by the cutoff condition, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\left(|v_h|^2 + v_z^2\right)^{\frac{\beta}{2(1+\beta)}} - |v_h|^\beta\leq \frac{1}{2}\left(v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}} + \frac{\beta}{2(1+\beta)}v_z^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}\right) - |v_h|^\beta\leq 0.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore,
    \begin{align*}
        &e^{-t}v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}e^{-\alpha|v_h|^\beta} \exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{2} |v|^{\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq (|x_h-v_ht| + v_z)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\\
        &\leq Ce^{-t} v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C$ depending on $\alpha$, which is similar to the equation right before \eqref{eq:g_LpvLqx_form}. In fact, for $t_0>0$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\left\|e^{-t} v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0,t_0)\times\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))} \\
        &= \left(\frac{1-e^{-qt_0}}{q}\right)^{1/q}\left\| v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{x_h}(\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for $1\leq q<\infty$ and
    \begin{align*}
        &\left\|e^{-t} v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h-v_ht| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^\infty_{t,x_h}([0,t_0)\times\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))} \\
        &= \left\| v_z^{1/q}\langle v\rangle^\delta \langle v_h\rangle^{-m}\mathbf{1}_{|v_h|\geq \left(|x_h| + v_z\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq 0}\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^\infty_{x_h}(\partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}
    \end{align*}
    for $q=\infty$. Therefore, if $m$ satisfies \eqref{eq:m_cond1} for $p=q=\infty$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta},\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\right\|_{L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}([0,\infty)\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n)}<\infty.
    \end{align*}
    Now, we can apply Proposition~\ref{prop:boltzmann_existence} and check that there exists a unique local-in-time solution of the Boltzmann equation in $L^\infty_{loc, t}([0, t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}, \delta}))$ space when $\kappa$ and $\beta$ satisfy
    \begin{align}\label{eq:kappa_range}
        \max\left\{0, \frac{2}{n-1}\kappa, \frac{2}{3}(1+\kappa)\right\}<\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}.
    \end{align}
    Furthermore, if $m$ is large enough such that it meets \eqref{eq:m_cond1} for given $1\leq p,q\leq \infty$, then it also satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|v_z^{1/q} w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta},\delta} g(t,x_h,v)\right\|_{L^p_v(\mathbb{R}^n, L^q_{t,x_h}([0,t]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+))}<\infty.
    \end{align*}
    If we further assume $1+\kappa\leq \frac{\beta}{1+\beta}$ when $n=2$, Proposition~\ref{prop:boltzmann_wellposed} shows that the Boltzmann solution is also in $L^\infty_{loc, t}([0, t_0), L^p_vL^q_x(w_{\frac{\alpha}{2},\frac{\beta}{1+\beta}, \delta}))$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
For $w_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}g(t, x_h, v)\in L^\infty_{t,x_h,v}\left([0, t^*_0]\times \partial \mathbb{R}^n_+\times \mathbb{R}^n\right)$, we extend it to $t\in [0,\infty)$ by making
    \begin{align*}
        \tilde{g}(t,x,v) = g(t, x_h, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\leq t^*_0}.
    \end{align*}
    Applying Proposition~\ref{prop:boltzmann_existence} for $\tilde{g}$, we find a unique solution of the Boltzmann equation $f$ in $L_{loc, t}^\infty([0,t_0), L^\infty_{x,v}(w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}))$ for some $0<t_0\leq t_0^*$. Finally, we employ Proposition~\ref{prop:boltzmann_wellposed} to get the $L_{loc, t}^\infty\left([0,t_0), L^p_vL^q_x(w_{\alpha, \beta,\delta}))\right)$ boundedness.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:BGK_contradiction}
    For the given initial data $f_0(x,v) = 0$ and inflow boundary data $g(t,x_h,v) = e^{-|v|^2}$, if $f(t,x,v)$ solves the BGK model, then it does not satisfy the second condition of Definition~\ref{def:BGK_sol_space}.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
We confine $0< t\leq 1$. We start by computing the $(\rho_g,u_g, T_g)(t,x)$. Using Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral}, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_rho_g_bound2}
        \begin{split}
            \rho_g(t,x) &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^n} e^{-t_b}e^{-|v|^2}1_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv = \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-|v_h|^2}\,dv_h\int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}}e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z\geq C_1 e^{-t}\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}},
            \intertext{and}
            \rho_g(t,x)&\leq C_2 \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for some constants $C_1$ and $C_2$. For $u_g$, we proceed with a similar calculation and obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \rho_g(t,x)u_{g,h}(t,x) &=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}v_h e^{-|v_h|^2}\,dv_h\int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}}e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z = 0,\\
        \rho_g(t,x)\left|u_{g,z}(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\right| &\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-|v_h|^2}\,dv_h\int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}}\left|v_z-\frac{z}{t}\right| e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z\leq C_3'\frac{1}{\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^2} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C_3'$, where $u_{g,h}$ and $u_{g,z}$ are horizontal and vertical components of $u_g$. It shows that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_u_g_bound2}
        u_{g,h}(t,x) = 0,\quad \left|u_{g,z}(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\right|\leq \frac{C_3}{\frac{z}{t} + 1}
    \end{align}
    for a constant $C_3$.
    
    For $T_g$, we write
    \begin{align*}
        n\rho_g(t,x)T_g(t,x)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}}\left(|v_h|^2 + |v_z - u_{g,z}|^2\right)e^{-|v|^2}1_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} |v_h|^2 e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\quad + \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty |v_z - u_{g,z}|^2 e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h.
    \end{align*}
    We first compute an upper bound of $T_g$. The first integral is easily bounded above by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} |v_h|^2 e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h\leq C\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}.
    \end{align}
    For the second integral, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty |v_z - u_{g,z}|^2 e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h&\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty 2\left(\left|v_z - \frac{z}{t}\right|^2 + \left|u_{g,z}-\frac{z}{t}\right|^2\right) e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h\\
        &\leq C\frac{1}{\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^3} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}
    \end{align*}
    by \eqref{eq:Appendix_u_g_bound2} and Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral}. Therefore, we conclude
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound2}
        n\rho_g(t,x)T_g(t,x)\leq C_5'\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}
    \end{align}
    for some $C_5'$. By a similar computation for \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound}, we compute the lower bound of $n\rho_g T_g$ by
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound3}
        n\rho_g(t,x)T_g(t,x)&\geq  \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} |v_h|^2 e^{-|v_h|^2} \int_{\frac{z}{t}}^\infty e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}} e^{-v_z^2}\,dv_z dv_h\geq C_4' \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}
    \end{align}
    for a constant $C_4'$. Combining all the macroscopic bound results \eqref{eq:Appendix_rho_g_bound2}, \eqref{eq:Appendix_u_g_bound2}, \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound2}, and \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_bound3}, we get
    \begin{align*}
        &C_1 e^{-t}\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}\leq \rho_g(t,x)\leq C_2\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}},\\
        &\left|u_g(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq \frac{C_3}{\frac{z}{t} + 1},\\
        &C_4\leq T_g(t,x)\leq C_5
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C_i$, $1\leq i\leq 5$.

    Now, we assume that there exists $0<t^*\leq 1$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:macro_contradiction}
        \begin{split}
            &|\rho-\rho_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}\rho_g(t,x),\\
            &\left|u-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|(t,x)\leq C_u\sqrt{T_g(t,x)},\\
            &|T-T_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}T_g(t,x)
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for some constant $C_u>0$ for $0< t\leq t^*$ and derive that $\rho(t,x)$ can not satisfy the condition. From the definition of the BGK solution, we have
    \begin{align*}
        f(t,x,v) &= e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b, x-vt_b, v)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq t_b} + \int_0^t e^{-(t-\tau)}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau\\
        &=e^{-t_b}e^{-|v|^2}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}} + \int_0^t e^{-(t-\tau)}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,d\tau,
    \end{align*}
    we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:contra_2}
        \rho(t,x) \geq \rho_g(t,x) + \int_0^t e^{-(t-\tau)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,dv d\tau.
    \end{align}
    We compute the integral of the local Maxwellian term and prove that it is much bigger than $\rho_g$.
    
    Since $(\rho,u,T)$ satisfy the relations, using \eqref{eq:macro_contradiction}, we can write
    \begin{align*}
        \mathcal{M}(f)(t,x,v)&= \frac{\rho(t,x)}{(2\pi T(t,x))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v-u(t,x)|^2}{2T(t,x)}\right)\\
        &\geq C\frac{\rho_g(t,x)}{(2\pi T_g(t,x))^{n/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{2\left(\left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2 + \left|u(t,x) - \frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2\right)}{T_g(t,x)}\right)\\
        &\geq C\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t}+1} e^{-\frac{z^2}{t^2}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}|^2}{C_4/4}\right).
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C$ depending on $C_i$ for $1\leq i\leq 5$, $C_u$, and dimension $n$. Using this lower bound, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^m \mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,dv\\
        &\geq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^m \frac{1}{\frac{z-v_z(t-\tau)}{\tau} +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z-v_z(t-\tau)}{\tau}\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + \left|v_z - \frac{z-v_z(t-\tau)}{\tau}\right|^2}{C_4/4}\right)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)>0}\,dv\\
        &=C\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} |v|^m \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - \frac{t-\tau}{\tau}v_z +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} - \frac{t-\tau}{\tau}v_z\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + \left|\frac{t}{\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_4/4}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\frac{t-\tau}{\tau}v_z<\frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &=C\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v_h|^2 + \left|\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2\right)^{m/2} \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - v_z +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + \left|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_4/4}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z<\frac{z}{t}}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    In the last two equalities, we used the change of variable $v_z\mapsto v_z + \frac{z}{t}$ and $v_z\mapsto \frac{\tau}{t-\tau}v_z$ in steps. We do the $v_h$-integral first and get
    \begin{align*}
        &\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^m \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - v_z +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + \left|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_T}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z<\frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &\geq C\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^m \frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - v_z +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{\left|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_T}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z<\frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z,
    \end{align*}
    where $C_T = C_4/4$.
    
    The sum of the exponents is given by
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z\right)^2 + \frac{\left|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_T} = \left(1 + \frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}\right)\left(v_z -\left(\frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}+1\right)^{-1}\frac{z}{t}\right)^2 + \frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, merging the two exponents and taking the change of variable
    \begin{align*}
        v_z\mapsto \left(1 + \frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}\right)^{-1/2}v_z + \frac{1}{1+\frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}}\frac{z}{t},
    \end{align*}
    we get
    \begin{align}
        &\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^m\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - v_z +1} e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z\right)^2}\exp\left(-\frac{\left|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z\right|^2}{C_T}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\notag\\
        &=\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^m\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} - v_z +1}\notag\\
        &\qquad \times \exp\left(- \left(1 + \frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}\right)\left(v_z -\left(\frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}+1\right)^{-1}\frac{z}{t}\right)^2\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\notag\\
        &\geq \frac{\tau}{t}\frac{\sqrt{C_T}}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t}\frac{\sqrt{C_T}}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\left|v_z+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}}}\frac{z}{t}\right|\right)^m \notag\\
        &\qquad \times \frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z}{t} - \frac{1}{\sqrt{1 + \frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}}}v_z +1}\exp\left(- |v_z|^2\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\notag.
    \end{align}
    We confine $0\leq \tau\leq t/2$ and $v_z\geq 0$. Then, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        &\frac{\tau}{t}\frac{\sqrt{C_T}}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t}\frac{\sqrt{C_T}}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\left|v_z+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}}}\frac{z}{t}\right|\right)^m\\
        &\qquad \times \frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}+1}\frac{z}{t} - \frac{1}{\sqrt{1 + \frac{t^2}{C_T(t-\tau)^2}}}v_z +1}\exp\left(- |v_z|^2\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\frac{C_T(t-\tau)^2}{t^2} + 1}}\frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\\
        &\geq \left(\frac{\tau}{t}\right)^{m+1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{C_T}}{\sqrt{C_T + 1}}\right)^{m+1}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T}{4}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right) \frac{1}{\frac{1}{\frac{C_T}{4}+1}\frac{z}{t}+1}\\
        &\qquad \times \int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(v_z + \frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{4}{C_T}}}\frac{z}{t}\right)^m\exp\left(- v_z^2\right)\mathbf{1}_{0\leq v_z< \frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T + 1}}\frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    When $\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T + 1}}\frac{z}{t}\geq 1$, then the final integral is bounded below by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(v_z + \frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{4}{C_T}}}\frac{z}{t}\right)^m\exp\left(- v_z^2\right)\mathbf{1}_{0\leq v_z< \frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T + 1}}\frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\geq C\left(1 + \frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{4}{C_T}}}\frac{z}{t}\right)^m.
    \end{align*}
    It shows that
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_0^t e^{-(t-\tau)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^m \mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,dv d\tau\\
        &\geq C\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T}{4}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right)\frac{1}{\frac{1}{C_T+1}\frac{z}{t}+1}\left(1 + \frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\frac{4}{C_T}}}\frac{z}{t}\right)^m \int_0^{t/2} e^{-(t-\tau)}\left(\frac{\tau}{t}\right)^{m+1}\,d\tau\\
        &\geq Ct\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T}{4}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right) \left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m-1}.
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C$ depending on $C_T$, $m$, and dimension $n$ when $0<t\leq 1$ and $\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{C_T + 1}}\frac{z}{t}\geq 1$. When we take $m=0$ and apply it to \eqref{eq:contra_2}, we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \rho(t,x)\geq \rho_g(t,x) + C\frac{1}{\frac{z}{t} +1}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{\frac{C_T}{4}+1}\frac{z^2}{t^2}\right)t.
    \end{align*}
    Since $C_T>0$, the density generated by $\mathcal{M}(f)$ is much bigger than $\rho_g$ when $\frac{z}{t}\gg 1$, so it can not satisfy \eqref{eq:macro_contradiction}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:suppressed_v_z}
    For $f_0(x,v) = 0$ and $g(t,x_h,v) = e^{-|v_h|^2 - |v_z|^\beta}$, if $0<\beta\leq 1$, then there exists a unique local solution $f$ satisfying the second condition of Definition \ref{def:BGK_sol_space}. In fact, we can take $g(t,x_h, v) = \phi(t,x_h,v_h)e^{-|v_z|^\beta}$ for $0<\beta\leq 1$ and any function $\phi(t,x_h, v_h)\geq 0$ satisfying
    \begin{align}\label{eq:suppressed_v_z_cond}
        \sup_{t\geq 0}\|(1+|v_h|^q)\phi(t,x_h, v_h)\|_{L^\infty_{x_h,v_h}}<\infty\quad \text{and}\quad \inf_{t\geq 0,\,0\leq \tau\leq t,\,x_h\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\phi(t-\tau, x_h-v_h\tau, v_h)\,dv_h>0
    \end{align}
    for some $q>n+1$.
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
We follow the proof strategy used in Sections~\ref{sec:Macro_quan} and \ref{sec:sol_construction}. We first compute the macroscopic fields $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$ generated by the damped free transport of the boundary data $g$, and then verify the \textit{a priori} stability estimate for the solution's macroscopic fields near $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$.
    
    By the change of variables $t\mapsto t+\frac{z}{v_z}$ and $x_h\mapsto x_h + v_h\frac{z}{v_z}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|(1+|v_h|^q)\phi(t-\frac{z}{v_z},x_h - v_h \frac{z}{v_z},v_h)\mathbf{1}_{v_z t\geq z}\right\|_{L^\infty_{x_h,v_h}} = \sup_{t\geq 0}\|(1+|v_h|^q)\phi(t,x_h, v_h)\|_{L^\infty_{x_h,v_h}}
    \end{align*}
    for all $z\geq 0$ and $v_z>0$. Therefore, applying the inequality of Perthame and Pulvirenti from \cite{PP1993} to $\phi(t-\frac{z}{v_z},x_h - v_h \frac{z}{v_z},v_h)\mathbf{1}_{t\geq \frac{z}{v_z}}$ and using the lower bound condition in \eqref{eq:suppressed_v_z_cond}, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:horizontal_int_bounds}
        \begin{split}
            &A_1\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\phi(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v_h)\,dv_h\leq A_2,\\
            &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|v_h|\phi(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v_h)\,dv_h\leq A_3,\\
            &A_4\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|v_h|^2 \phi(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v_h)\,dv_h\leq A_5
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for some uniform constants $A_i$, $1\leq i\leq 5$ for all $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$, $v_z>0$, and $t\geq t_b$.
    
    Next, we compute $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$. For this, we confine $0<t\leq 1$. By the definition of $g(t,x_h,v)$, we write the $\rho_g$ integral by
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\frac{z}{v_z}}e^{-v_z^\beta}\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\phi(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v_h)\,dv_h dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    By \eqref{eq:horizontal_int_bounds} and Lemma \ref{lem:exp_integral}, we estimate $\rho_g$ by
    \begin{align*}
        C_1e^{-t}\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta} e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}\leq \int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv&\leq C_2\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta} e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C_1$ and $C_2$. By similar calculations, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}|v_h|g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\leq C_3'\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta} e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}},\\
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left|v_z-\frac{z}{t}\right|g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\leq C_3' \left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{2(1-\beta)} e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}.
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C_3'$. At this stage, we can state that there exist constants $C_i$ for $1\leq i\leq 3$ such that
    \begin{align}\label{eq:Appendix_macro_bound1}
        \begin{split}
            &C_1\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{1-\beta}e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^\beta}\leq \rho_g(t,x) \leq C_2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{1-\beta}e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}},\\
            &\left|u_g(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq C_3\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    for $0<t\leq 1$. To compute $T_g$, we first bound the integral for $\left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2$ weight as
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2 g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &=\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left(|v_h|^2 + \left|v_z-\frac{z}{t}\right|^2\right) g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
        &\leq C_5'\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{3(1-\beta)}e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C_5'$ employing \eqref{eq:horizontal_int_bounds} and Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral}. We need to use the weight $|v-u_g(t,x)|^2$ to compute $T_g$. For the upper bound, we easily get
    \begin{align}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}|v-u_g(t,x)|^2 g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\notag\\
        &=\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left|\left(v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)-\left(u_g(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)\right|^2 g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\notag\\
        &\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left(\left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2+\left|u_g(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^2\right) g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\notag\\
        &\leq 2 (C_5' + C_3^2 C_2)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{3(1-\beta)}e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}\label{eq:Appendix_T_g_upper}.
    \end{align}
    For the lower bound, we take the $z$-component of the weight $|v-u_g(t,x)|^2$. As $u_g$ satisfies \eqref{eq:Appendix_macro_bound1}, if we take $v_z\geq \frac{z}{t} + (C_3+1)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}$, we have
    \begin{align}
        &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left|v-u_g(t,x)\right|^2 g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\notag\\
        &\geq \int_{\mathbb{R}^n}e^{-t_b}\left|v_z-u_{g,z}(t,x)\right|^2 g(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_z\geq \frac{z}{t} + (C_3+1)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}}\,dv\notag\\
        &\geq e^{-t}\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{2(1-\beta)}\int_{\frac{z}{t} + (C_3+1)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}}^\infty e^{-v_z^\beta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}} \phi(t-t_b,x_h-v_ht_b,v_h)\,dv_h dv_z\notag\\
        &\geq C_4'\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{3(1-\beta)} e^{-\frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta}}\label{eq:Appendix_T_g_lower}
    \end{align}
    for a constant $C_4'$. In the final inequality, we assume $\frac{z}{t}\geq 1$ and use Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} with Lemma~\ref{lem:beta_pert} to get
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{z}{t} + (C_3+1)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}\right)^\beta&\leq \frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta} + \beta (C_3+1)\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta}\left(\frac{z}{t}\right)^{\beta-1}\\
        &\leq \frac{z^\beta}{t^\beta} + 2^{1-\beta}\beta (C_3+1).
    \end{align*}
    We divide \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_upper} and \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_lower} by $\rho_g$ in \eqref{eq:Appendix_macro_bound1} to get the upper and lower bounds of $T_g$. If $\frac{z}{t}\leq 1$, the $v_z$ integral is bounded below by a constant, so we again get the lower bound \eqref{eq:Appendix_T_g_lower}.
    
    Adding the bounds of $T_g$ to \eqref{eq:Appendix_macro_bound1}, we prove that there exist constants $C_i$ for $1\leq i\leq 5$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        &C_1\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{1-\beta}e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^\beta}\leq \rho_g(t,x) \leq C_2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{1-\beta}e^{-\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^\beta},\\
        &\left|u_g(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq C_3\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta},\\
        &C_4\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{2(1-\beta)}\leq T_g(t,x) \leq C_5\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{2(1-\beta)}
    \end{align*}
    for $0< t\leq 1$. Note that it is safe to add $1$ in the exponent of $\rho_g$ since $0<\beta\leq 1$. 
    
    Now, as in Proposition \ref{prop:M[f]_integral}, we want to bound the integral of local Maxwellian $\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)$ generated by $(\rho_g, u_g, T_g)$. In general, we claim that if the macroscopic fields $(\rho, u, T)$ of $f$ satisfy
    \begin{align*}
        &C_1\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1}e^{-\alpha\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^\beta}\leq \rho(t,x) \leq C_2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1}e^{-\alpha\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^\beta},\\
        &\left|u(t,x)-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|\leq C_3\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)^{1-\beta},\\
        &C_4\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{2(1-\beta)}\leq T(t,x) \leq C_5\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{2(1-\beta)},
    \end{align*}
    for some $0\leq \alpha\leq 1$ and $m_1\in\mathbb{R}$, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int_res}
        \sup_{0<\tau\leq t}\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^{m_2}\mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,dv\leq C_{m_1,m_2}(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^\beta}
    \end{align}
    for each $m_2\geq 0$. If $\tau = t$, then it is an integral of $\mathcal{M}(f)(t,x,v)$ and we directly get \eqref{eq:appendix_int_res}. Thus, we assume $0<\tau<t$. Applying the chain of change of variables used in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:M[f]_integral}, we can write the integral of the local Maxwellian as
    \begin{align*}
        &\frac{t}{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left|v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|^{m_2} \mathcal{M}(f)(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)> 0}\,dv\\
        &\leq C\frac{t}{t-\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v_h|^{m_2} + \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2} \right)\frac{e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)^\beta}}{(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{n(1-\beta) - m_1}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + |\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv.
    \end{align*}
    We perform the $v_h$-integral first and get
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{\mathbb{R}^n} \left(|v_h|^{m_2} + \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2} \right)\frac{e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)^\beta}}{(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{n(1-\beta) - m_1}}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_h|^2 + |\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv\\
            &\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)^\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z\\
            &\quad + C\int_{\mathbb{R}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)} e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)^\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{4C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{v_z< \frac{z}{t}}\,dv_z.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    The final integrals closely resemble the $v_z$-integrals over the regions $A_i$ in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:M[f]_integral}, with $x_h=v_h(t-\tau)$. Therefore, we can apply the same proof technique for Proposition \ref{prop:M[f]_integral} to these integrals. For completeness, we provide the details.
    
    We first claim that
    \begin{align*}
        e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)^\beta}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)&\leq Ce^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1 + \frac{t}{t-\tau}|v_z|)^\beta}\\
        &\leq Ce^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^\beta}
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C$. Indeed, when $0<\beta<1$, we can choose $\beta'>0$ satisfying $\beta = \frac{\beta'}{1+\beta'}$ and directly apply Lemma~\ref{lem:concaten} to get the bound. If $\beta = 1$, by direct computation, we have
    \begin{align*}
        e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5}\right) &= e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t} + 1)}\exp\left(-\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z - 4\alpha\frac{t-\tau}{t}C_5\right)^2}{8C_5} + 2\alpha^2\frac{(t-\tau)^2}{t^2}C_5\right)\\
        &\leq \exp\left(2\alpha^2 C_5\right) e^{-\alpha(\frac{z}{t} + 1)}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, for the final integrals in \eqref{eq:appendix_int}, it is enough to bound the integrals by
    \begin{align}
        &\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{z}{t}} (\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)},\label{eq:appendix_int_1}\\
        &\int_{-\infty}^{\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)} \exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{8C_5(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}\label{eq:appendix_int_2}
    \end{align}
    to get the result \eqref{eq:appendix_int_res}. For each integral, we decompose the integral domain into three parts: $\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}\leq v_z<\frac{z}{t}$, $-2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)\leq v_z< \frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}$, and $v_z\leq -2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)$ and estimate the integral. For notational convenience, we set $C_T = 8C_5$ in the exponent of the integrals.
    
    When $\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}\leq v_z<\frac{z}{t}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} (\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq \int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} (\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &=\frac{t-\tau}{t}\int_{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{z}{t}} (\frac{z}{t} - \frac{t-\tau}{t}v_z + 1)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z,
        \intertext{and}
        &\int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq \int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &=\frac{t-\tau}{t}\int_{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - \frac{t-\tau}{t}v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq \frac{t-\tau}{t}\int_{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{t}{t-\tau}\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{z}{t} - \frac{t-\tau}{t}v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}v_z^{m_2}\exp\left(-\frac{|v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z.
    \end{align*}
    Regardless of the polynomial terms, due to Lemma~\ref{lem:exp_integral} and $0<\beta\leq 1$, we have exponential decay about $\frac{z}{t}$ and $\frac{t}{t-\tau}$ as the result of the integral. Therefore, we have
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int_3}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} (\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}\\
            &\int_{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}}^{\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    
    If $-2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)\leq v_z<\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}$, then we have
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t} + \frac{1}{2}\leq \frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\leq 3\frac{z}{t} + 3.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, we obtain
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int_4}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{-2\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
            &\leq C\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\int_{-\infty}^\infty \exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(3\frac{z}{t} + 3)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
            &\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    For the second integral, by a similar reason,
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int_5}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{-2\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)}^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{z}{t}} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)} \exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
            &\leq C\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)}\int_{-\infty}^\infty \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(3\frac{z}{t} + 3)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
            &\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Hence, we get the desired result for each \eqref{eq:appendix_int_1} and \eqref{eq:appendix_int_2} in this case.
    
    Finally, suppose $v_z\leq -2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)$. In this case, we have $|v_z|\leq \frac{z}{t}-v_z + 1\leq 2|v_z|$. Using these bounds, we split the exponential function into half and get
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{-\infty}^{-2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)} \left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq C\int_{-\infty}^{-2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)} |v_z|^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}|v_z|^{\beta}\right)^2}{2^{2(1-\beta) }C_T}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}2^\beta \left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{\beta}\right)^2}{2^{2(1-\beta)}C_T}\right)\int_{-\infty}^{-2} |v_z|^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}|v_z|^{\beta}\right)^2}{2^{2(1-\beta)}C_T}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}2^\beta \left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{\beta}\right)^2}{2^{2(1-\beta)}C_T}\right).
    \end{align*}
    By a similar calculation, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_{-\infty}^{-2\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)} \exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\\
        &\leq C\left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}\right)^{m_2}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left(\frac{t}{t-\tau}2^\beta \left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)^{\beta}\right)^2}{2^{2(1-\beta)}C_T}\right).
    \end{align*}
    Since the exponential decay can suppress $\frac{z}{t}$ and the time singular term $\frac{1}{t-\tau}$ for arbitrary order, we get
    \begin{align}\label{eq:appendix_int_6}
        \begin{split}
            &\int_{-\infty}^{-2\left(\frac{z}{t} + 1\right)} \left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 + (m_2-1)(1-\beta)}\exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)},\\
            &\int_{-\infty}^{-2\left(\frac{z}{t}+1\right)} \left(\frac{\tau}{t-\tau}|v_z|\right)^{m_2}\left(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1\right)^{m_1 -(1-\beta)} \exp\left(-\frac{|\frac{t}{t-\tau}v_z|^2}{C_T(\frac{z}{t} - v_z + 1)^{2(1-\beta)}}\right)\,dv_z\leq C\frac{t-\tau}{t}(\frac{z}{t} + 1)^{m_1 + m_2(1-\beta)}.
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
    Combining the integral estimates \eqref{eq:appendix_int_3}, \eqref{eq:appendix_int_4}, \eqref{eq:appendix_int_5}, and \eqref{eq:appendix_int_6}, we conclude \eqref{eq:appendix_int_1} and \eqref{eq:appendix_int_2}, and so \eqref{eq:appendix_int_res}.
    
    Now, we have versions of Proposition~\ref{prop:M[f]_integral} and Corollary~\ref{prop:bound} (for $\alpha = 0$ and general $m_1\in\mathbb{R}$) for our boundary data. At this stage, we define a solution space $\Omega'_{\gamma, t_0}$ of the BGK model for our inflow boundary data as follows: For $\gamma\geq 0$, $t_0>0$, and given inflow boundary data $g(t,x_h,v)$, $\Omega'_{\gamma, t_0}$ is the collection of functions $f(t,x,v)\geq 0$ for $0\leq t\leq t_0$ such that
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item it satisfies
        \begin{align*}
            \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0< t\leq t_0}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+}\int_{v\in \mathbb{R}^n}\left(1+\left(\frac{z}{t}\right)^{2\gamma} + \left(v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)^2\right)f(t,x,v)\,dv<\infty
        \end{align*}
        and $f(0,x,v) = 0$.
        \item For the macroscopic fields $(\rho, u, T)$ of $f$, there exists a constant $C_u>0$ such that
        \begin{align*}
            &|\rho-\rho_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}\rho_g(t,x),\\
            &\left|u-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right|(t,x)\leq C_u\sqrt{T_g(t,x)},\\
            &|T-T_g|(t,x)\leq \frac{1}{2}T_g(t,x)
        \end{align*}
        for almost every $0<t\leq t_0$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+$.
    \end{enumerate}
    Starting from \eqref{eq:appendix_int_res}, we can check that the solution of the BGK model with the inflow boundary data satisfies the conditions for $\Omega'_{1-\beta, t_0^*}$ for some $t_0^*>0$ and $C_u = \frac{C_3+1}{\sqrt{C_4}}$, modifying the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:apriori}. Furthermore, for $f_1,f_2\in \Omega'_{1-\beta, t_0^*}$, we can reproduce Proposition~\ref{prop:maxwell} by
    \begin{align*}
        &\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}\left(1+\left(\frac{z}{t}\right)^{2(1-\beta)} + \left(v-\frac{z}{t}\hat{z}\right)^2\right)\left|\mathcal{M}(f_1)-\mathcal{M}(f_2)\right|(\tau, x-v(t-\tau), v)\mathbf{1}_{z-v_z(t-\tau)>0}\,dv\\
        &\leq C\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^n_+}\int_{\mathbb{R}^n}\left(1+\left(\frac{z}{\tau}\right)^{2(1-\beta)} + \left(v-\frac{z}{\tau}\hat{z}\right)^2\right)|f_1-f_2|(\tau,x,v)\,dv
    \end{align*}
    for almost every $0<\tau\leq t$ and all $0<t\leq t_0^*$ from the pointwise estimate of $(\rho, u, T)$ in the second condition. Finally, following the contraction argument in Theorem~\ref{thm:BGK_sol}, we get a unique solution $f$ in $\Omega'_{1-\beta, t_0}$ for some $t_0$, which satisfies the second condition of Definition \ref{def:BGK_sol_space}.
